20	## Glossary (campaign terms — domain terms live in `CONTEXT.md`)
21	
22	- **Campaign** — this whole program of work: one architecture, N increments.
23	- **Increment** — one unit of work with its own session, spec, implementation, review,
24	  commit, and archive. Numbered I0–I14.
25	- **Wave** — an ordered group of increments (0–4); increments within a wave may be
26	  reordered if the ledger records why, waves may not.
27	- **Block** — a `Bnn` region of `main()` per `BLOCKMAP.md`; the stable unit of scope
28	  assignment.
29	- **Core package** — the new importable `psh/` package holding infrastructure (Tier 1).
30	- **Gateway** — `psh/gateway.py`, the single module through which every Terminus/WP-CLI/
31	  Drush subprocess flows; the future Pantheon-API replacement seam.
32	- **Façade** — `script_context.py` (`sc`), the stable API surface that checks and plugins
33	  import; implementations move, the façade's names do not break.
34	- **Contract** — the per-phase guaranteed `site_context` keys (CLAUDE.md table), which
35	  this campaign turns into a machine-readable **contract registry**.
36	- **Hook DAG** — the per-phase topological ordering of hooks derived from declared
37	  `consumes`/`produces` keys, validated fatally at startup.
38	- **Ratchet** — the lint/type regime: broad ruff+pyright rules applied as a hard gate to
39	  moved/new modules immediately, the remnant grandfathered until I14.
40	- **Remnant** — whatever remains of the original script at any point mid-campaign.
41	- **Shim** — the thin committed `./pantheon-sitehealth-emails` entry script that calls
42	  `psh.cli.main()` after I0.
43	- **Ledger** — `LEDGER.md`; how increment N learns what N−1 actually did.
44	- **Behavior bar** — the tiered definition of which observable behavior may change (§8).
45	- **Invariant** — a named property no increment may alter (§9).
46	
47	**MUST** = required, violation fails review. **NEVER** = prohibited, violation fails
48	review. **SHOULD** = required unless the increment spec states why not. **MAY** =
49	allowed, at the implementer's judgment.

51	## 1. Goal and non-goals
52	
53	**Goal.** Modularize the 4,752-line main script into (a) a `psh/` core package of
54	infrastructure modules, (b) self-registering `check/` packages for every notice/section
55	emitter, and (c) the existing `plugin/` integrations — taking full advantage of the hook
56	system — while the four e2e goldens stay byte-identical, the per-phase contract is
57	honored, and the non-U-M path keeps working. End state: `main()` is a ~250–400-line
58	orchestrator; every U-M-specific behavior lives in `umich` packages; the whole tree
59	passes the broadened ruff+pyright configuration.
60	
61	**Non-goals** (exhaustive; each is either declined or deferred with reasoning in §15):
62	replacing terminus with the Pantheon API; implementing parallel site processing; any new
63	report content (CSV attachment, cached-% column, env-lock section); SendGrid; refreshing
64	goldens or recorded fixtures; changing what any check reports (except the named bug
65	fixes in I1).
66	
67	## 2. Decision record (exhaustive — from the approved brainstorm)
68	
69	| # | Decision | Choice | Why |
70	|---|---|---|---|
71	| D1 | Pantheon API | **Seam only**: gateway module now, transport swap post-campaign | Swapping transports mid-campaign invalidates terminus fixtures + goldens in every touched increment |
72	| D2 | Lint/type broadening | **Ratchet in-campaign** (§13) | Code is cleaned exactly once, as it moves; bar fixed in I0 so it never shifts |
73	| D3 | Behavior bar | **Tiered, config renames allowed** (§8) | Cleanest final schema; production config edited once, at I14, with a migration table |
74	| D4 | Increment granularity | **Fine: 15 increments** | Safest for session/context limits; split-never-compress backstop (§12) |
75	| D5 | Target architecture | **Three-tier split** (§3) | Infrastructure ≠ report content ≠ data source; fulfills "full advantage of the frameworks" |
76	| D6 | Hook flexibility | **Phases stay; hooks declare consumes/produces; per-phase DAG validated at startup** (§4) | Keeps every existing hook/test valid; a phase-less key scheduler rewrites everything for no added power |
77	| D7 | `--only-warn` plan rec | **In campaign** (I7) | Small, no golden impact, existing TODO |
78	| D8 | Parallel-ready | **Design constraint only** (§3.4) | Near-free now; actual parallelism stays a README TODO |
79	| D9 | CSV attachment / cached-% / env-lock | **README TODO** | Each changes rendered email → golden churn mid-campaign |
80	| D10 | Packaging | **Real package + thin shim** (I0; console-script dropped — see LEDGER I0 amendment 1) | Dissolves the extension-less-script problem; normal imports for tests/pyright/ruff |

167	### 3.4 Parallel-ready constraint (D8)
168	
169	Per-site work MUST be a function of `(site, config, db_session, site_context)`: no new
170	module-level mutable state; run-scoped accumulators live only in `RunState`. This is a
171	review criterion from I2 onward, not a parallelism implementation.
172	
173	### 3.5 The `sc` façade
174	
175	Checks and plugins import **only** `sc` (and their own package). `sc` keeps every name
176	listed in CLAUDE.md's runtime-exposed block, re-exporting from `psh/` modules as they
177	move. NEVER remove or rename an `sc` attribute mid-campaign; additions are fine. The
178	house-rules test suite gains an assertion that every documented `sc` name exists (I2).
179	
180	## 4. Phases, hooks, and the DAG
181	
182	Phases stay the coarse spine: `setup`, `site_pre`, `site_post_traffic`, `site_post_dns`,
183	`site_post_gather`, `site_pre_render`, plus **new** `run_finish` (fired inside
184	`finish_run` before artifacts are written, receiving the `RunState`; for future run-level
185	artifact hooks — no consumer at introduction, like `site_pre_render` was). Dotted
186	plugin-defined events are unchanged.
187	
188	From I4, `add_hook` requires two new entries per hook: `consumes` and `produces` — each
189	a (possibly empty) list of contract-key names. Validation at module-load completion
190	(exhaustive fatal conditions):
191	
192	1. A consumed key that nothing produces (neither core's registry for that phase or an
193	   earlier phase, nor another hook) → fatal.
194	2. Two producers of the same key → fatal (one owner per key; PD#1 — a silent overwrite
195	   is a silent failure).
196	3. A cycle among same-phase hooks → fatal.
197	4. A hook consuming a key first produced in a *later* phase → fatal.
198	5. A missing `consumes`/`produces` entry on any hook → fatal (no legacy mode; I4
199	   retrofits all in-repo hooks in the same change).
200	
201	`invoke_hooks` orders same-phase hooks topologically (producers before consumers;
202	registration order breaks ties, so existing behavior is preserved where no edges exist).
203	The permanent test `tests/integration/test_hook_dag.py` loads **all** real check/plugin
204	packages and asserts the DAG builds — the "future changes can never make the DAG
205	impossible" guarantee — and a unit suite proves each fatal condition actually fires
206	(PD#14: the validator must be shown able to go red).
207	
208	```
209	 module load ──► collect hooks ──► per phase: build edges (produces→consumes)
210	                                        │
211	                              cycle? unknown key? dup producer? ──► fatal exit (named error)
212	                                        │ ok
213	                                topo order stored ──► invoke_hooks uses it
214	```
215	
216	**Contract registry.** `psh/modules.py` holds the machine-readable registry: phase →
217	keys core stuffs (today's CLAUDE.md table, verbatim). Core's stuffing code is checked
218	against it in tests; CLAUDE.md's table gains a line saying the registry is authoritative.
219	New contract keys added by increments (exhaustive for this campaign): `envs` (I8, at
220	`site_pre`), `add_on_updates` + `wp_smell`/`drush_smell`/`composer_smell` (I9/I10, at
221	`site_post_gather`), plan/cost keys `current_plan`, `recommended_plan`, `plan_costs`,
222	`savings` (I7, at `site_pre_render`). Each addition updates registry + CLAUDE.md table +
223	ledger in the same increment.
224	
225	**Hook-produced keys (I10).** A hook MAY produce keys of its own — declared in its
226	`produces`, validated for duplicate producers, cycles, and phase position by the same
227	conditions 1–4 above. Such keys are **DAG-declared, not registry-owned**: they are present
228	only when the producing hook actually ran (absent when its gate failed or its package is
229	disabled), so consumers read them with `.get()`, and they are **NOT** part of the
230	guaranteed per-phase contract (the "new contract keys" list above stays exhaustive for
231	registry-owned keys only). The campaign's first are `drupal_multisite` /
232	`drupal_multisite_smell`, produced by `check.drupal.multisite` at `site_post_dns` and read
233	by `main()` after the phase (I10; see LEDGER I10 amendment 2).
234	
235	## 5. Configuration
236	
237	Principles: one section per feature, named for the operator's mental model; every
238	relocated check gets an `enabled` flag under `[Check.<name>]`, **default true** —
239	relocating code MUST NOT silently disable a check that runs unconditionally today.
240	U-M-only checks additionally require `[UMich].enabled` (existing `umich_enabled()`
241	rule). `gate_disabled_sections()` semantics (nested `enabled`, children dropped) apply to
242	`[Check.*]` unchanged.
243	
244	Example (actual TOML, the shape I8 introduces — illustrative of the family, exhaustive
245	for `check/pantheon/`):
246	
247	```toml
248	[Check.pantheon]
249	enabled = true          # frozen-site, live-env, upstream-updates, PHP-EOL checks
250	```
251	
252	New keys land in final shape as introduced (I3 onward). Renames/moves of *existing* keys
253	happen once, in I14, which MUST deliver: the old→new migration table in
254	`docs/config-migration.md`, a rewritten `sample-pantheon-sitehealth-emails.toml`, and
255	exact edit instructions for the production config repo. Until I14, every existing
256	production key keeps working unchanged.
257

259	
260	Reused as-is: `TrafficRow`, `OverageProtectionRow`, `DnsFacts`, `FetchResult`,
261	`SiteContext`. Introduced (exhaustive):
262	
263	| Type | Increment | Shape |
264	|---|---|---|
265	| `GatewayResult` | I2 | NamedTuple `(result, errors, fatal)` replacing the anonymous 3-tuples |
266	| `Notice` | I3 (class) → adopted per increment | frozen dataclass: `severity` (StrEnum alert/warning/info), `code` (unique — registry test), `html`, `text`, `short`, `icon`, `order`; `SiteContext.add_notice` accepts `Notice` or legacy dict; dict form retired in I14 |
267	| `PlanInfo` / `PlanCatalog` | I7 | typed view over `[Pantheon.plan_info]` |
268	| `RunState` | I13 | dataclass holding `all_warnings`, `site_results`, `site_savings`, `emails_sent`, reconnect counters |
269	
270	House-style tuple annotations (`-> (str, str, bool)`) are replaced with real annotations
271	in every module as it moves — never fixed in place in the remnant (one pass per line,
272	D2). CLAUDE.md's house-style note is updated in I14 when the last one dies.
273	
274	## 7. Per-increment obligations
275	
276	Every increment MUST (this list is exhaustive and lives only here; increment specs cite
277	it): (1) start by reading `CAMPAIGN.md`, `LEDGER.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `BLOCKMAP.md` rows in
278	scope; (2) follow `prompts/implementation-standards.md` (subagent-driven, test-first,
279	`psh-implementer`/`psh-reviewer`); (3) replace house styles in moved code (§6);
280	(4) verify — not assume — every claim in comments/docs it moves or writes;
281	(5) update tests in the same change; (6) update README/docs/CLAUDE.md for what moved;
282	(7) update auto-memory where a durable fact changed; (8) append its ledger entry
283	(§12); (9) preserve every invariant (§9); (10) end with `/code-review`, a full
284	`./run-tests`, and one checkpoint commit including its `development/` folder.

287	
288	| Surface | Rule | Until |
289	|---|---|---|
290	| Rendered emails (4 goldens) | NEVER change (byte-identical) | end of campaign |
291	| `-results.json` / `-notices.csv` / `-run.json` structure (keys, row shape) | NEVER change | end of campaign |
292	| Notice csv *values* | MAY change only in I1 (named bug fixes), I7 (`its-recommends-plan` savings-field format, D-i7-5 — amendment), I9 (wp-smell precedence when theme-list and OCP-probe stderr co-occur without favicon stderr — see LEDGER I9), and I14a (B51 deletion — originally scheduled for I12 "if past its date"; the Aug-2026 marker date had not passed at I12 nor by 2026-07-23, and the user approved deleting it early at I14a rather than carrying it post-campaign — amendment, LEDGER I14a) | — |
293	| stdout / console / error messages | MAY improve freely | — |
294	| Config: existing keys | NEVER break | I14 (renames with migration table) |
295	| Config: new keys | MUST land in final schema shape | — |
296	| Exit codes, resume semantics, artifact write gates | NEVER change | end of campaign |

298	## 9. Named invariants (exhaustive; NEVER violated by any increment)
299	
300	1. Four e2e goldens byte-identical (`./run-tests` proves it; refresh is forbidden — an
301	   existing golden going red is a defect in the increment, PD#14).
302	2. Per-phase data contract: existing keys never removed/renamed/retyped; additions only.
303	3. Non-U-M path works: non-U-M golden green; no new un-gated U-M content (I1 *removes*
304	   the one known leak).
305	4. Run lifecycle: single `except BaseException` flush path; `abort_reason`'s three
306	   outcomes; artifacts dropped-site rule; notices appended before send; resume-point
307	   next-site-after-email rule; soft-wrapped copy-pasteable commands.
308	5. DB: `db_retry` retries whole idempotent units only; `db_retryable` predicate
309	   unchanged; the read-release commit in the loaders stays (guarded by
310	   `test_load_traffic_rows_releases_the_connection`).
311	6. Rich console rules: escape untrusted text; production width reproduced in tests.
312	7. Test safety interlock (`run_program` forbidden flags) never bypassed or weakened.
313	8. Column-0 `f"""` notice literals move **verbatim** — never re-indented; `git diff -w`
314	   is not acceptable evidence for any change touching them.
315	9. Checks/plugins import only `sc`; `sc` names never removed mid-campaign (§3.5).
316	10. Recorded fixtures are not regenerated (`terminus-cdnchange/` is hand-maintained and
317	    `--record` must not run).
318	11. `--create-tables`/`--update`/`--import-older-metrics` phase-gating rules (CLAUDE.md
319	    table) unchanged.

321	## 10. Known-bug inventory → I1
322	
323	The five bugs and the dead code listed in `BLOCKMAP.md` §Bugs (composer-smell
324	nesting+variable; shared `php-eol` code; `site_results` omission; un-gated U-M portal
325	URLs; duplicate `annual-bill` code — B51 handled as: distinct code now, scheduled
326	deletion when its Aug-2026 date passes, ledgered to I12; dead code deleted). Each fix is
327	test-first with the test shown red on the old behavior. **Verified 2026-07-17**: the
328	goldens contain zero `php-eol`/`wp-smell`/`drush-smell`/`composer-smell`/`annual-bill`
329	occurrences and the golden fixtures report PHP 8.2, so none of these fixes can touch a
330	golden:
331

347	Wave 1: I2 gateway ──► I3 config ──► I4 hooks+DAG ──► I5 DB

350	Wave 4: I14a structural ──► I14b ratchet ──► I14c notice ──► I14d closing

384	
385	## 12. Coordination protocol
386	
387	**Ledger entry template** (append per increment, and for any amendment):
388	
389	```markdown
390	## I<N> — <slug> (<date>, commit <sha>)
391	- Moved: <blocks/functions actually moved>
392	- Deviations from CAMPAIGN.md: <none | what + why>
393	- Contract/config/sc additions: <keys/names>
394	- Discovered tasks: <each with disposition: fixed here | I<M> | README TODO>
395	- Open questions for next increment: <…>
396	```
397	
398	**Discovered-task disposition rules** (canonical): fits current increment's scope and
399	<~30 min → fix now, note in ledger; belongs to a later increment → ledger it against
400	that increment (the increment's spec author MUST read these); major/risky/scope-widening
401	→ README TODO with a sentence of context. Nothing is carried in memory or chat — if it
402	is not in the ledger or README, it does not exist (PD#9).
403	
404	**Session flow per increment:** read the §7 documents → write the increment SPEC.md in
405	`development/<date>-mod-I<N>-<slug>/` citing CAMPAIGN.md sections by number →
406	`superpowers:writing-plans` → subagent-driven implementation → `/code-review` → full
407	`./run-tests` → per-task commits, each green; the increment's final commit includes the dev
408	folder → `/archive-session` → ledger entry.
409	
410	## 13. Lint/type ratchet
411	
412	Mechanism (as shipped by I0; see LEDGER I0 amendment 2): TWO ruff configs —
413	`pyproject.toml` `[tool.ruff.lint]` keeps the narrow PD-rule set running everywhere
414	including the remnant, and `ruff-broad.toml` carries `select = ["ALL"]` minus a
415	justified ignore list, with `extend-exclude` grandfathering exactly the remnant
416	(`psh/_legacy.py`) and not-yet-moved files; each increment deletes its
417	files from the grandfather list, and the two configs merge at I14. pyright runs in
418	`./run-tests` from I0 via `[tool.pyright]` (standard mode, `psh/` minus `_legacy.py`),
419	ratcheting toward strict as typed code moves in. The four existing narrow rules (`E722`, `BLE001`, `S105`, `S106`) remain global
420	throughout — they mechanize PD#2/PD#6 and are never grandfathered. No
421	`target-version` pin (CLAUDE.md: it masks the 3.12-only syntax detection).
422	
423	Baselines measured 2026-07-17 (I0 re-measures and pins both in its spec):
424	
425	```
426	$ ./run-tests --fast --llm   (tail)
427	LLM_SUMMARY passed=727 failed=0 error=0 skipped=1 xfailed=0 xpassed=0
428	25 snapshots passed.
429	
430	$ uvx ruff check --isolated --statistics .
431	26  F541  f-string-missing-placeholders
432	 8  E741  ambiguous-variable-name
433	 4  E713  not-in-test
434	 3  F841  unused-variable
435	 2  F401  unused-import
436	 1  E402  module-import-not-at-top-of-file
437	 1  E712  true-false-comparison
438	Found 45 errors.
439	```
440	
441	(README's "~55" ruff and "39" pyright figures are stale/unverified claims; I0 replaces
442	them with measured numbers. pyright was not measured in planning — no pyright binary in
443	the dev container yet; installing it is I0 scope.)
444	
445	## 14. Risk / control table
446	
447	| Risk | Control |
448	|---|---|
449	| Re-indented column-0 notice literals silently change emails | Invariant 8; goldens as tripwire; AST/token comparison, never `git diff -w` |
450	| conftest rework silently drops tests | I0 gate: identical collected-test count (727 passed / 1 skipped / 2 deselected baseline) before and after |
451	| Long-range `main()` local coupling breaks a move | BLOCKMAP produces/consumes; each increment spec lists exactly which locals cross its boundary |
452	| Session/context overrun mid-increment | Fine granularity (D4); split-never-compress; commits only at increment completion |
453	| Architecture drift across 15 sessions | This document frozen; amendments only via ledger; specs cite section numbers |
454	| Hidden hook-order dependencies surface in I4 | I4 audits every hook; real dependencies become explicit DAG edges |
455	| Ratchet churn on moved code | Rules fixed at I0; cleaning is part of each move |
456	| Goldens blind to stdout/artifacts | Invariants 4–5 name the artifact/abort test suites as the cover; artifact structure frozen until campaign end |
457	| Implementer sessions lack context | `psh-implementer`/`psh-reviewer` carry the read list; specs name seams (Spine spec bar); §7 reading list |
458	| Two annual-bill notices / removal date passes mid-campaign | Explicitly scheduled: codes split in I1, deletion decision in I12 (kept — date unpassed), resolved at I14a (user-approved early deletion; §8 amendment, LEDGER I14a) |
459	
460	## 15. NOT in scope (reasoning preserved so it is never re-litigated)
461	
462	- **Terminus → Pantheon API swap** — D1; post-campaign project against `psh/gateway.py`.
463	- **Parallel site processing** — D8; constraint only; README TODO remains.
464	- **CSV data attachment, cached-% column, env-lock section** — D9; each becomes a small
465	  post-campaign change (env-lock: a ~50-line `check/` package) once goldens may move.
466	- **SendGrid, secrets-handling completion, portal traffic capture, daily alerts,
467	  accessibility/security/Cloudflare scores, AI recommendations, dependency updates,
468	  terraform-infra** — pre-existing README TODOs, untouched by this campaign.
469	- **Approach B (library-only) and C (everything-is-a-hook)** — rejected in brainstorm:
470	  B fails the "full advantage of the frameworks" goal; C forces infrastructure into
471	  optional-content clothing.
472	- **Golden/fixture refreshes** — forbidden (Invariants 1, 10).
473

475	
476	§13 outputs are the campaign-start baseline (run and pasted 2026-07-17). Every
477	increment's definition of done re-runs `./run-tests` at increment end — the full suite
478	when the live tier's credentials are available in the session, otherwise `--fast` with a
479	ledger note saying the live tier was skipped — and MUST reproduce goldens
480	byte-identically. I0 additionally records the collected-test count gate.
481

485	2. Has every DAG fatal condition been demonstrated red at least once?
486	3. Do the contract registry and CLAUDE.md table agree (test-enforced)?
487	4. Is any `sc` re-export now consumed by nobody (dead façade surface)?
488	5. Is the `.py` symlink still needed for anything beyond the shim? If not, note in
489	   CLAUDE.md; if yes, say for what.
490	6. Are all ledger items resolved (done, scheduled, or README TODO)?
491	7. Has the production config repo received and applied the migration instructions?
492	8. Do README, CLAUDE.md, docs/, and memory reflect the final architecture (no stale
493	   line-number or module references)?
494	9. Were any invariants amended mid-campaign, and is each amendment ledgered?

### ↳ Tool result

```
1	"""The Notice type and its code registry (CAMPAIGN.md §6).
2	
3	A typed, frozen replacement for the ad-hoc notice dicts.  Pure: imports nothing from script_context,
4	so the sc facade and every psh/ module can import it without a cycle; checks/plugins reach
5	Notice/Severity via sc.  Adoption is per-increment (CAMPAIGN.md §6); the dict form is retired in I14.
6	"""
7	import dataclasses
8	from enum import StrEnum
9	
10	
11	class Severity(StrEnum):
12	    ALERT = "alert"
13	    WARNING = "warning"
14	    INFO = "info"
15	
16	
17	@dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True)
18	class Notice:
19	    """One report notice.  `code` is the stable unique slug (registry-enforced) that maps to the
20	    notices-CSV code field; `html` is the report-body HTML, `text` its plaintext (empty -> derived by
21	    SiteContext.add_notice via html2text, as the dict form does); `short` is the one-line summary;
22	    `icon` empty -> filled from `severity`; `order` places the notice ('prepend'/'first' -> front)."""
23	
24	    severity: Severity
25	    code: str
26	    html: str
27	    short: str = ""
28	    text: str = ""
29	    icon: str = ""
30	    order: str = "append"
31	
32	
33	class DuplicateNoticeCodeError(RuntimeError):
34	    """Raised when a notice code is registered twice.  A shared code across two notice types is the
35	    exact class of bug I1 fixed by hand (BLOCKMAP §Bugs 2/5); the registry makes it a loud
36	    import-time failure instead of a silent CSV collision."""
37	
38	
39	class NoticeRegistry:
40	    """Declare-once registry of notice codes.  Each notice type registers its code once at import; a
41	    re-used code raises DuplicateNoticeCodeError.  Registration is import-time metadata (like
42	    sc.substitutions/sc.hooks), not per-run/per-site state (CAMPAIGN.md §3.4)."""
43	
44	    def __init__(self) -> None:
45	        self._codes: dict[str, str] = {}
46	
47	    def register(self, code: str, *, description: str = "") -> str:
48	        if code in self._codes:
49	            raise DuplicateNoticeCodeError(
50	                f"notice code {code!r} is already registered "
51	                f"(existing: {self._codes[code]!r}); codes must be unique."
52	            )
53	        self._codes[code] = description
54	        return code
55	
56	    def codes(self) -> frozenset[str]:
57	        return frozenset(self._codes)
58	
59	
60	registry = NoticeRegistry()
61
```

```
def no_primary_domain_notice(site, custom_domains, primary_domain, is_multisite):
```

```
    # (development/2026-07-13-db-connection-resilience/SPEC.md 3.6).  Written by psh.db.db_retry;
    # read by finish_run/abort_run; absorbed into RunState at campaign I13.
    db_reconnect_failures_by_site: dict[str, int] = dataclasses.field(default_factory=dict)

    def record_site_notices(self, notices: list[dict], contacts: str) -> None:
        """Append a completed site's notice csv rows, contacts inserted at field 2.

        BEFORE the send, not after: a Ctrl-C between send_message() and this loop -- a window
        that includes smtp_connection.quit(), a network round-trip -- used to set
        site_emailed=True and so advance the resume point PAST this site, and its notices
        then never reached {ymd}-notices.csv on any run, even though its owner had the email
        describing them.  Appending first downgrades that to at worst a duplicate CSV row on
        a re-run, which docs/resuming-interrupted-runs.md already documents as tolerable.
        """
        for n in notices:
            fields = n["csv"].split(",")
            fields.insert(1, contacts)
            self.all_warnings.append(",".join(fields))


class ResumeSiteNotFoundError(Exception):
    """--resume-from named a site not present in the org site list."""


def sites_from_resume_point(sorted_site_names: list[str], resume_from: str) -> list[str]:
    """
    Return the suffix of sorted_site_names starting at resume_from (inclusive).

    sorted_site_names is the already-sorted list of org site names; resume_from is the
    --resume-from value.  Raises ResumeSiteNotFoundError if resume_from is absent, so that a
    typo becomes a fatal error rather than degrading into "silently skip every site".
```

```
66	def html_to_text(html: str) -> str:
67	    """Render notice/news HTML to the plaintext used in the text/plain email part.
68	
69	    A FRESH HTML2Text per call, deliberately: the instance is stateful, and sharing one
70	    across calls made every notice render differently from its siblings -- the run's FIRST
71	    notice came out in a different link style from all the others, and html2text's reference
72	    counter climbed across the whole run instead of restarting.
73	
74	    Links are reference-style, and html2text numbers them per handle() call.  An email body
75	    is many notices concatenated (email_template.txt loops over notice.text), so one message
76	    can contain several "[1]" labels -- one per notice.  That is an ACCEPTED trade-off, not an
77	    oversight: each notice's footnote definitions immediately follow its own block
78	    (links_after_para), so every label sits next to its URL and reads unambiguously.  The
79	    alternative -- inline links -- is worse in this content: with wrap_links=False any
80	    paragraph containing a link stops wrapping (250+ character lines in a plaintext email),
81	    and with wrap_links=True html2text splits long URLs mid-string and breaks them.
82	    `inline_links` is the flag html2text actually honors; `reference_links` alone only flips
83	    it during the first handle(), which is what made the first notice of a run come out in a
84	    different style from all the others.
85	
86	    Equivalent to:
87	      html2text --protect-links --images-to-alt --unicode-snob --reference-links
88	                --no-wrap-links --links-after-para --wrap-list-items --pad-tables file.html
89	    """
90	    text_maker = html2text.HTML2Text()
91	    text_maker.protect_links    = True
92	    text_maker.images_to_alt    = True
93	    text_maker.unicode_snob     = True
94	    text_maker.reference_links  = True
95	    text_maker.inline_links     = False   # what reference_links=True actually needs; see above
96	    text_maker.wrap_links       = False
97	    text_maker.links_after_para = True
98	    text_maker.wrap_list_items  = True
99	    text_maker.pad_tables       = True
100	    return text_maker.handle(html)
101	
102	
103	class SiteContext(dict):
104	    """
105	    Per-site report context.
106	
107	    A dict subclass so that `site_context['notices' | 'sections' | 'attachments' | 'site']`
108	    access is unchanged throughout the code and the Jinja templates, while the object also owns
109	    the mutators for its collections (`add_notice`/`add_notices`/`add_section`/`add_attachment`).
110	    Constructed once per processed site; notices/sections/attachments accumulate through the
111	    per-site pipeline (including `check` hooks) and are consumed by the template render.
112	    """
113	
114	    def __init__(self, site: dict):
115	        super().__init__(site=site, notices=[], sections=[], attachments=[])
116	
117	    def add_notice(self, notice) -> None:      # notice: Notice | dict
118	        """Add a notice (Notice or legacy dict), filling icon (from 'type'), plaintext 'text' (via
119	        html2text), and honoring order ('prepend'/'first' -> front).  A Notice is projected to the
120	        legacy dict first (dict form retired in I14, CAMPAIGN.md §6)."""
121	        if isinstance(notice, Notice):
122	            notice = self._notice_to_dict(notice)
123	        if 'message' not in notice:
124	            console.print(f'[bold red]ERROR: Notice is missing the "message" key: {notice}')
125	            sys.exit(1)
126	        if 'icon' not in notice:
127	            notice['icon'] = icon[notice['type']]
128	        if 'text' not in notice:
129	            notice['text'] = html_to_text(notice['message'])
130	        order = notice.get('order', 'append')
131	        if order in ('prepend', 'first'):
132	            self['notices'].insert(0, notice)
133	        else:
134	            self['notices'].append(notice)
135	
136	    def _notice_to_dict(self, notice: Notice) -> dict:
137	        """Project a Notice onto the legacy notice dict.  csv is built from the site name + code (the
138	        two-field form; extra-csv-field notices stay dicts until their adopting increment).  icon /
139	        text / non-default order are set only when present so the stored dict is byte-identical to the
140	        legacy one and add_notice's fill logic supplies icon/text identically."""
141	        d = {
142	            "type": str(notice.severity),
143	            "csv": f"{self['site']['name']},{notice.code}",
144	            "short": notice.short,
145	            "message": notice.html,
146	        }
147	        if notice.icon:
148	            d["icon"] = notice.icon
149	        if notice.text:
150	            d["text"] = notice.text
151	        if notice.order != "append":
152	            d["order"] = notice.order
153	        return d
154	
155	    def add_notices(self, notices: list) -> None:
156	        """Add each notice dict returned by a builder (wp_error/drush_error/check_*module)."""
157	        for notice in notices:
158	            self.add_notice(notice)
159	
160	    def add_section(self, section: dict) -> None:
161	        """Add a report section (rendered into the email body)."""
162	        self['sections'].append(section)
163	
164	    def add_attachment(self, attachment: dict) -> None:
165	        """Add an attachment (e.g. an inline image referenced by cid)."""
166	        self['attachments'].append(attachment)
167	
168	
169	def msgid_domain() -> str:
170	    """
171	    The domain used for inline-image Content-IDs (make_msgid), from [Email].msgid_domain.
172	    Single source of the default so the core script and the umich check package don't each
173	    hard-code it.
174	    """
175	    return config.get('Email', {}).get('msgid_domain', 'webservices.umich.edu')
176	
177	
178	def smtp_username() -> str:
179	    """
180	    The effective SMTP / dry-run username: the --smtp-username option if given, else
181	    [SMTP].username (which is dropped when [SMTP] is disabled, per the section-gating rule),
182	    else the empty string.  Used both for SMTP login and for the dry-run To: operator copy.
183	    """
184	    if options.smtp_username is not None:
185	        return options.smtp_username
186	    return config.get('SMTP', {}).get('username', '') or ''
```

```
"""Syrupy pins of the check/pantheon notice bodies -- the forward byte-identity guard for
the verbatim move (campaign I8; move-time evidence is the extracted-block diff in the
task report, the I2 precedent)."""
import datetime
import types

import pytest
from helpers.checkload import load_check_module
from helpers.dnsfake import recording_console

pytestmark = pytest.mark.integration

SITE = "bus-occb"


def _notice(reset_sc, mod_func, ctx):
    mod_func(ctx)
    assert len(ctx["notices"]) == 1
    return ctx["notices"][0]


def test_frozen_notice_snapshot(psh, reset_sc, request, monkeypatch, snapshot):
    recording_console(monkeypatch, reset_sc)
    mod = load_check_module(psh, "pantheon", "frozen", "pantheon_frozen_snap", request)
    ctx = reset_sc.SiteContext({"name": SITE, "frozen": True})
    n = _notice(reset_sc, mod.check_frozen_site, ctx)
    assert n["message"] == snapshot
    assert n["text"] == snapshot
    assert n["short"] == snapshot


def test_no_live_env_notice_snapshot(psh, reset_sc, request, monkeypatch, snapshot):
    recording_console(monkeypatch, reset_sc)
    mod = load_check_module(psh, "pantheon", "live_env", "pantheon_live_snap", request)
    ctx = reset_sc.SiteContext({"name": SITE})
    ctx["envs"] = {"live": {"initialized": False}}
    n = _notice(reset_sc, mod.check_live_env, ctx)
    assert n["message"] == snapshot
    assert n["text"] == snapshot
    assert n["short"] == snapshot


class _FrozenNow(datetime.datetime):
    @classmethod
    def now(cls, tz=None):
        return cls(2026, 7, 1, 12, 0, tzinfo=tz)


@pytest.mark.parametrize(("iso", "variant"), [
    ("2026-06-28T00:00:00+00:00", "info"),

])

    recording_console(monkeypatch, reset_sc)
    mod = load_check_module(psh, "pantheon", "updates", f"pantheon_upd_snap_{variant}", request)

        [{"datetime": iso, "message": "Update WordPress to 6.6", "author": "Pantheon"}],
===AMBR===
```

```
"""U-M annual-billing notice (campaign I12, from B50; B51 deleted I14a), a site_pre_render hook.

The notice is published as a HOOK-PRODUCED site_context key (CAMPAIGN.md §4, the I10
drupal_multisite precedent) -- `annual_bill_upcoming` -- NOT via `add_notice`.  main()'s
`sort_notices_and_subject` reads it with `.get()` and inserts it at the front of the
*rendered* notice list; it NEVER enters site_context["notices"].  This is deliberate and
load-bearing (SPEC I12 §2.2): the pre-campaign code inserted it straight into the
render-only `sorted_notices` local, so its csv rows have never reached `all_warnings` /
`-notices.csv`.  Using `add_notice` would break that quirk, so the absence of a csv path
here is a feature, not an omission.  (B51, the "ITS is in the process of billing"
companion notice, was deleted at campaign I14a -- user-approved early deletion, before its
Aug-2026 removal marker; CAMPAIGN.md §8 as amended 2026-07-23.)

Registered inside check/umich/__init__.py's [UMich].enabled guard, so the `umich_enabled()`
test is subsumed by the registration gate (the oidc_login/drupal_ua precedent).
"""

import script_context as sc


def build_annual_bill_upcoming_notice(site_name, plan_name, annual_bill, shortcode, portal_site_id):
    """The contract-year-end "will be billed July 1" alert (BLOCKMAP B50)."""
    return {
        "type": "alert",
        "icon": "&#x1F4B5;",  # dollar banknotes
        "csv": f"{site_name},annual-bill,{annual_bill},{shortcode}",
        "short": f"${annual_bill:,.2f} will be billed to shortcode {shortcode} on July 1",
        "message": f"""
                <p style="background-color: #f8d7da; padding: 1em; border: 2px solid #58151c;">
                    On July 1, ${annual_bill:,.2f} will be billed to shortcode <strong>{shortcode}</strong>
                    when ITS runs its billing process.  This charge will be for a
                    full year (July 1 - June 30) of Pantheon hosting on the {plan_name} plan for the site
                    <strong>{site_name}</strong>.
                </p>
                <p>Please see if a different plan would be better:</p>
                <ul>
                    <li><a href="#estimated-costs">Estimated Plan Costs for {site_name}</a> (see the table, below)</li>
                    <li><a href="https://docs.pantheon.io/guides/account-mgmt/plans/resources">Pantheon Plan Resources</a></li>
                    <li><a href="https://its.umich.edu/computing/web-mobile/pantheon/pricing">U-M Pantheon pricing</a></li>
                </ul>
                <p>Do you want to change to a different plan or have use a different shortcode?</p>
                <ul>
                    <li>
                        <a href="https://admin.webservices.umich.edu/sites/{portal_site_id}/plan/">Change the plan for {site_name}</a>.
                        Changes must be made by the end of the day on June 29 for the July 1 annual billing.
```

```
import pytest

import script_context as sc
from psh.notice import Notice, Severity

pytestmark = pytest.mark.unit


def test_notice_projects_to_legacy_dict():
    html = "<p>hi</p>"
    from_notice = sc.SiteContext({"name": "s1"})
    from_notice.add_notice(
        Notice(severity=Severity.ALERT, code="no-domains",
               short="no domains connected", html=html, text="hi")
    )
    from_dict = sc.SiteContext({"name": "s1"})
    from_dict.add_notice(
        {"type": "alert", "csv": "s1,no-domains",
         "short": "no domains connected", "message": html, "text": "hi"}
    )
    assert from_notice["notices"] == from_dict["notices"]   # full dict equality (both lack 'order')


def test_notice_text_defaults_via_html2text():
    html = "<p>hello world</p>"
    ctx = sc.SiteContext({"name": "s1"})
    ctx.add_notice(Notice(severity=Severity.INFO, code="x", short="s", html=html))
    assert ctx["notices"][0]["text"] == sc.html_to_text(html)
```

## Assistant

I've read CAMPAIGN.md (all 495 lines), the I3/I14a/I14b ledger entries, and inventoried the actual conversion surface. Facts on the ground:

- **37 notice-dict producers** across 20 files (`psh/gateway.py`, `psh/gather.py` ×9, `psh/cli.py`, `psh/plans.py`, `check/{pantheon,wordpress,drupal,dns,umich,cloudflare,addon_updates,pantheon_cdn_change}/`), all with a uniform key set (`type, [icon], csv, short, message, [text]`). No producer sets `order`; news items are a separate path (no dict literals in-tree).
- Storage stays dicts (templates, `sort_notices_and_subject`, `record_site_notices` all read `n["csv"]`/`n["message"]`), so the ~107 `.ambr` snapshots and hook-level tests that read `site_context["notices"]` are untouched; only tests calling *builders* directly need repointing (~10 files).
- Two non-`add_notice` paths need handling: `check/umich/annual_billing.py`'s hook-produced `annual_bill_upcoming` key and `psh/cli.py`'s `no_primary_domain_notice`.
- The reserved §6 amendment is unavoidable: 22 of 37 csvs carry extra fields (`turned-off,{name}`, `updates-info,{n},{days}`, `wp-error,{code},{json}`, `no-primary-domain,` with a *trailing empty field*, the dns `",".join(hostnames)` forms, `its-recommends-plan,{cur},{rec},{savings:.2f}`).

Three decisions shape the SPEC before I write it:

### ↳ Tool result

```
1	# New-Feature Standards
2	
3	A **standards overlay** for the `superpowers:brainstorming` skill. The skill drives the
4	*process* (explore context → ask one question at a time → propose 2–3 approaches → present
5	the design in sections → write & review the spec → hand off to `writing-plans`). This file
6	defines the *bar* and the *judgment* to apply inside that flow. Where they overlap, the
7	skill owns the process; this file owns the standards.
8	
9	> **Read `prompts/directives.md` first.** It is the Spine: the Posture, the 14 Prime
10	> Directives, the Engineering Preferences, and the spec quality bar — the single copy.
11	> This file adds only what is specific to designing a feature; it does not restate a rule
12	> from there. Directives are cited here **by number**.
13	
14	## Two things the skill does not tell you to do
15	
16	1. **Verify load-bearing claims.** Independently confirm the facts a design rests on —
17	   from the prompt, documentation, code, and anything I assert in this session — rather
18	   than trusting them. Confirm them against the **authority**, not against an artifact's
19	   appearance: a directory listing is not a plugin manifest, a tool's shape is not its
20	   documented contract, and a number you recall is not a number you measured. This is where
21	   designs here fail most often.
22	2. **Surface expansion opportunities, one at a time.** Hold my feature description as the
23	   baseline and make it bulletproof. *Separately*, present each expansion you see as its
24	   own `AskUserQuestion` so I can cherry-pick. Neutral posture: state the opportunity, its
25	   effort, and its risk, then let me decide. Accepted expansions join the plan's scope;
26	   rejected ones go to an explicit **"NOT in scope"** list, with the reasoning preserved so
27	   a later session doesn't re-litigate them. Keep the *picker* cheap — one question per
28	   expansion. Once I accept one whose shape isn't settled, that's when to go deep with the
29	   `/grilling` skill.
30	
31	## Selecting a solution
32	
33	The skill already generates 2–3 approaches; this is the rubric for judging them. Evaluate
34	each option against the factors below using a **checklist backed by quoted evidence** —
35	from the Spine's standards *and* from industry best practice — **not** a self-graded number.
36	For each factor, note how important it is relative to the others. Refine any option that
37	fails a factor and re-evaluate (up to three passes). Select on the weight of evidence across
38	factors; use professional judgment to break ties and secure the best outcome.
39	
40	Factors: **Correctness · Completeness · Ability to implement · Maintainability ·
41	Robustness/fragility · Clarity · Security · Testing · Observability.**
42	
43	## Where the spec goes
44	
45	Create the spec/plan and other documents produced under `development/`, in the same
46	subdirectory as the prompt if the prompt came from a file, or in a new subdirectory named
47	with a proper date and slug if it did not. This is instead of putting the files under
48	`docs/superpowers`.
49	
50	**Commit the spec before implementation begins.** Without a committed baseline there is no
51	diff, and "did this section shrink?" or "what changed since review?" become unanswerable —
52	which is PD#14 (§ Spine) applied to the document itself.
53	
54	## This project's context
55	
56	Read `CLAUDE.md` for the conventions a design must respect here:
57	
58	- Self-registering `plugin/` (data sources/integrations) and `check/` (report sections)
59	  packages, wired through the ordered `sc.PHASES` seams and their per-phase data contract.
60	  New integrations go in a package, not the core.
61	- **Tests**: add/adjust the right tier under `tests/`; run with `./run-tests`
62	  (`--fast` for the offline loop). Respect the safety interlock — no
63	  `--all`/`--for-real`/live `--create-tables` in tests.
64	- **Prefer the Pantheon API over `terminus`** for new code unless `terminus` is clearly
65	  better (missing endpoints, materially simpler/cleaner, better results).
66	- Keep institution-specific logic behind config flags / the `umich` plugin+check packages
67	  so the tool stays reusable by other institutions.
68
```

### ↳ Tool result

```
1	# Directives
2	
3	**The Spine.** The single copy of this project's Posture, Prime Directives, Engineering
4	Preferences, and spec quality bar. Every overlay in `prompts/` layers a *process* on top of
5	these; none of them restates a rule from here. An overlay MAY cite a directive by number.
6	
7	> **Why one copy.** These rules previously lived in two files and **drifted** — PD#11 gained
8	> a `/domain-modeling` mandate in one copy and not the other, and neither said which
9	> governed. The adversarial reviewer, dispatched with fresh context precisely to be
10	> independent, read the stale one. Two sources of truth is not redundancy; it is a bug with
11	> a delay fuse.
12	
13	## Posture
14	
15	You are a senior software architect (12+ years of Python CLI tooling, REST APIs, WebOps,
16	and WordPress/Drupal hosting) whose judgment produces better solutions and higher-quality
17	code than 99% of developers.
18	
19	You are not here to rubber-stamp my intention or reach for the quickest/easiest/obvious
20	design. You are here to make the work extraordinary, catch every landmine before it
21	explodes, and ensure that what ships, ships at the highest possible standard.
22	
23	## Prime Directives
24	
25	1. **Zero silent failures.** Every failure mode must be visible — to the system, the team,
26	   and the user. A failure that can happen silently is a critical defect.
27	2. **Every error has a name.** Never "handle errors." Name the specific exception class,
28	   what triggers it, what catches it, what the operator/user sees, and whether it's tested.
29	   Catch-all handling (`except Exception`, bare `except`) is a defect; ruff detects it, so
30	   spend your attention on the naming above instead.
31	3. **Data flows have shadow paths.** Every flow has a happy path plus three shadows: nil
32	   input, empty/zero-length input, and upstream error. Trace all four for every new flow.
33	4. **Interactions have edge cases.** Map them: interrupted run (Ctrl-C mid-site), slow or
34	   failing Terminus/WP/Drush/API/SMTP calls, session expiry, stale DB or cached state.
35	5. **Observability is scope, not an afterthought.** New code paths need structured logging
36	   at the right verbosity (`-v`/`-vv`/`-vvv`), failures surfaced actionably to the operator,
37	   and clear dry-run visibility. New report sections, notices, and runbook steps are
38	   first-class deliverables, not post-launch cleanup.
39	6. **Security is not optional.** New code paths get threat-modeled. Route secrets through
40	   config `<{secret env …}>` substitutions, never read them from the environment directly;
41	   ruff and the house-rules test detect the mechanical half, not the threat model.
42	7. **Runs are not atomic.** A run can die partway — a site fails, a session expires, SMTP
43	   drops. Plan for partial states: idempotent DB writes, resumability (`--resume-from`),
44	   safe re-runs, and the `--for-real`/dry-run gate as the primary blast-radius control.
45	8. **Diagrams are mandatory in the design.** No non-trivial flow ships undiagrammed in the
46	   spec — ASCII art for every new data flow, state machine, processing pipeline, dependency
47	   graph, and decision tree. **In code, a diagram is REQUIRED only where the flow is
48	   non-local** (spans files, packages, or phase seams). Where a diagram exists in a comment
49	   or docstring, updating it is part of changing the flow it describes; a stale diagram is
50	   worse than none.
51	9. **Everything deferred is written down.** Vague intentions are lies.
52	10. **Optimize for the 6-month future, not just today.** If the plan solves today's problem
53	    but creates next quarter's nightmare, say so explicitly.
54	11. **Terminology stays clear and consistent** — within the new design and across the
55	    existing codebase. Fix any terminology problems you find. Use the `/domain-modeling`
56	    skill to do it: challenge terms that conflict with the glossary, sharpen fuzzy ones, and
57	    write each resolution into `CONTEXT.md` **the moment it crystallizes** — don't batch
58	    them. `CONTEXT.md` is a domain glossary and nothing else; implementation detail belongs
59	    in `CLAUDE.md` (`docs/agents/domain.md` states the split). The `superpowers` host does
60	    not know about this skill — this directive is what invokes it, so don't wait to be asked.
61	12. **Scrap it and do this instead.** You have standing permission to table a problematic
62	    part — or the whole original design — when there's a fundamentally better approach. I'd
63	    rather hear it now.
64	13. **Update memory** with relevant findings and decisions.
65	14. **Your instruments can lie.** A test, golden, fixture, shim, counter, log line, or
66	    metric is code, and can be silently wrong. **A green check is a claim, not evidence,
67	    until it has been shown capable of going red on the condition it guards.** Corollaries
68	    this generalizes: watch the test fail for the *right reason*; reproduce production's
69	    console width rather than a comfortable one; prove every shim actually runs; count what
70	    *healed*, not what was *attempted*; an existing golden going red is a signal, never
71	    refreshed to green.
72	
73	> **On #14.** It is not theory. Every instrument named in it has been the bug here: the e2e
74	> suite reported green while testing a program with **every check disabled**; a second
75	> `sitecustomize.py` meant one silently never ran, and a `not in`-shaped assertion passed
76	> against a run that did nothing; `db_retry` reported "1 reconnect" on the run that aborted
77	> *because nothing reconnected*; a test console wider than production's hid the 80-column
78	> wrap that **re-mailed every site owner**. Applies at design time too — to a new counter,
79	> artifact, or notice — not only in tests.
80	
81	## Engineering Preferences
82	
83	- **DRY** — flag repetition aggressively.
84	- **Well-tested is non-negotiable** — I'd rather have too many tests than too few. But
85	  each test must serve a real purpose / provide benefit, don't test just for the sake
86	  of an increased coverage metric.
87	- **"Engineered enough"** — neither under-engineered (fragile, hacky) nor over-engineered
88	  (premature abstraction, needless complexity).
89	- **More edge cases, not fewer** — thoughtfulness over speed.
90	- **Explicit over clever.**
91	- **Right-sized diff** — favor the smallest design diff that cleanly expresses the change,
92	  but don't compress a necessary rewrite into a minimal alteration. If the foundation is
93	  broken, invoke Prime Directive #12.
94	
95	## Spec & internal-doc quality bar
96	
97	- Glossary at top; every term of art used exactly once per concept; no typos in terms,
98	  keys, or names.
99	- MUST / SHOULD / MAY / NEVER defined and used consistently.
100	- Every gate/precondition in one canonical table; no negation chains in prose.
101	- Every list marked exhaustive or illustrative; no open-ended denylists.
102	- Every referenced file has a path a fresh session can resolve.
103	- Config shown as an actual file snippet, not notation — and **merged with what the file
104	  already contains**, never as a fragment a reader would paste over the real thing.
105	- Each rule stated once and cross-referenced elsewhere (DRY).
106	- Intent ("why") attached to every rule, requirement, or decision that looks arbitrary.
107	- Acceptance criteria = exact commands + expected output, **run and pasted**, never
108	  summarized. Run them *before* submitting: an unrun acceptance suite is PD#14 exactly.
109	- **Seams under test are named and agreed — in the spec, before any implementation.** This is
110	  load-bearing, not a nicety: implementation is test-first (`mattpocock-skills:tdd`, per
111	  `prompts/implementation-standards.md`), that skill forbids a test at an unconfirmed seam,
112	  and implementer subagents have fresh context and cannot ask me. **The spec is the only
113	  place a seam can be agreed.** For each behavior: name the seam, prefer an existing one
114	  (`run_terminus`, `psh.dns_classify.resolve`, `httpseam.fetch`/`sleep`, `egress.probe`, the
115	  pure-helper defs), and use the highest one that reaches the behavior. Fewer seams is better.
116	  Where a core `main()` change has no seam above the e2e golden, either name the pure helper
117	  to extract — that extraction is in scope — or state explicitly why no seam is worth making.
118	  Silence is not an option a reviewer should accept.
119	- "Tests are load-bearing" NEVER-block included; golden/fixture regeneration requires a
120	  reviewed diff.
121	- Checklists with quoted evidence, never self-graded numeric gates.
122	- Reviewer runs with fresh context and sees only the artifact.
123	- Human approval gates are structural STOPs (exact-phrase unlock), not list items.
124	- Stable rules live in `CLAUDE.md`; other documents carry only task-specific material.
125	- Closing audit questions queued for after implementation.
126
```

```
def check_wordpress_plugin(  # noqa: PLR0913 -- moved verbatim, signature unchanged (Task 4 brief): one input per notice ingredient, pinned by the papc/sessions/cloudflare_cms call sites
    site: str,
    installed_plugins: list,
    name: str,
    display_name: str,
    url: str,
    reason: str,
) -> list:
    notices = []
    if not isinstance(installed_plugins, list):
        return notices  # this error should already have been handled by our caller, so skip additional work

    installed = [p for p in installed_plugins if p["name"] == name]

    if len(installed) == 0:
        sc.console.print(
            f":exclamation: [bold red] ATTENTION: {site} does not have the {display_name} plugin installed."
        )
        notices.append(
            {
                "type": "warning",
                "icon": "&#x26A0;",  # warning sign
                "csv": f"{site},not-installed,{name}",
                "short": f"install the {name} plugin",
                "message": f'<p>The <a href="{escape_url(url)}">{html.escape(display_name)}</a> WordPress plugin needs to be installed:</p><p>{html.escape(reason)}</p>',
                "text": f"The {display_name} WordPress plugin\n<{url}>\nneeds to be installed: {reason}",
            }
        )
        return notices

    if len(installed) > 1:
        sc.console.print(
            f":exclamation: [bold red] ATTENTION: {site} has more than one {display_name} plugin installed."
        )
        notices.append(
            {
                "type": "info",
                "icon": "&#x1F50E;",  # magnifying glass
                "csv": f"{site},multiple-installed,{name}",
                "short": f"plugin {name} installed multiple times",
                "message": f'<p>The <a href="{escape_url(url)}">{html.escape(display_name)}</a> WordPress plugin is installed multiple times.</p>',
                "text": f"The {display_name} WordPress plugin\n<{url}>\nis installed multiple times.",
            }
        )

    plugin = installed[0]
    if "status" not in plugin or plugin["status"] not in (
        "active",
        "active-network",
        "must-use",
    ):
        sc.console.print(
            f":exclamation: [bold red] ATTENTION: {site} has the {display_name} plugin installed but it is not active."
        )
        notices.append(
            {
                "type": "warning",
                "icon": "&#x26A0;",  # warning sign
                "csv": f"{site},turned-off,{name}",
                "short": f"activate plugin {name}",
                "message": f'<p>The <a href="{escape_url(url)}">{html.escape(display_name)}</a> WordPress plugin needs to be activated:</p><p>{html.escape(reason)}</p>',
                "text": f"The {display_name} WordPress plugin\n<{url}>\nneeds to be activated: {reason}",
            }
        )

    return notices
```

```
@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def reset_sc(psh):
    """Restore script_context's mutable globals (deep-copied) around every test.
===CHECKLOAD===
"""Load a check/ package (or one module of it) standalone, without importing the dash-named
main script.

A check module that uses relative imports (`from . import chain`) cannot be loaded with a bare
SourceFileLoader: Python needs a parent package with a __path__ first.  These helpers register a
probe package in sys.modules, then load under it -- the pattern established by
tests/integration/test_check_cloudflare_init.py.
"""
import importlib.util
import sys
from pathlib import Path


def _package_dir(psh, package, base="check"):
    return Path(psh.__file__).resolve().parents[1] / base / package


def _purge(probe):
    """Remove the probe package AND every submodule the import machinery created under it.

    monkeypatch.delitem(..., raising=False) on a key that does not exist yet records NO undo
    entry -- so submodules created later by `from . import chain` would survive teardown and be
    "restored" into the next test under a parent that no longer exists.  Purge by prefix instead
    of guessing a submodule list.  (This is the same class of bug as the reset_sc escape_url leak
    already recorded in this repo.)
    """
    for name in [m for m in sys.modules if m == probe or m.startswith(probe + ".")]:
        del sys.modules[name]


def load_check_package(psh, package, probe, request, base="check"):
    """Execute <base>/<package>/__init__.py as `probe` -- i.e. RUN its hook registration.

    `base` defaults to "check"; pass base="plugin" to load a plugin/ package instead --
    same probe-package mechanics, the two trees are siblings (CLAUDE.md)."""
    pkg_dir = _package_dir(psh, package, base)
    _purge(probe)
    request.addfinalizer(lambda: _purge(probe))
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(
        probe, str(pkg_dir / "__init__.py"), submodule_search_locations=[str(pkg_dir)])
    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    sys.modules[probe] = module
    spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    return module


def load_check_module(psh, package, module, probe, request):
    """Load ONE module out of check/<package>/ WITHOUT running the package __init__.py (so no
    hooks are registered).  Relative imports inside it resolve against the real directory: a
    package shell with __path__ set (and NOT exec_module'd) is enough for `from . import chain,
    pantheon` and `from .model import Finding`.
    """
    pkg_dir = _package_dir(psh, package)
    _purge(probe)
    request.addfinalizer(lambda: _purge(probe))
    pkg_spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(
        probe, str(pkg_dir / "__init__.py"), submodule_search_locations=[str(pkg_dir)])
    pkg = importlib.util.module_from_spec(pkg_spec)
    pkg.__path__ = [str(pkg_dir)]          # a package shell: __path__ WITHOUT exec_module
    sys.modules[probe] = pkg
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(
        f"{probe}.{module}", str(pkg_dir / f"{module}.py"))
    mod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    sys.modules[f"{probe}.{module}"] = mod
    spec.loader.exec_module(mod)
    return mod
===INIT-TEST===
"""check/pantheon registration + [Check.pantheon] gating (campaign I8, SPEC D-i8-6).

Default is ENABLED: relocating code must not silently disable a check that ran
unconditionally before (CAMPAIGN.md section 5)."""
import pytest
from helpers.checkload import load_check_package
from helpers.dnsfake import recording_console

pytestmark = pytest.mark.integration


def test_registers_hooks_when_config_is_silent(psh, reset_sc, request):
    reset_sc.config = {}
    load_check_package(psh, "pantheon", "pantheon_init_probe", request)
    assert [h["name"] for h in reset_sc.hooks["site_pre"]] == [
        "check.pantheon.frozen.check_frozen_site",
        "check.pantheon.live_env.check_live_env",
    ]
    assert [h["name"] for h in reset_sc.hooks["site_post_gather"]] == [
        "check.pantheon.updates.check_upstream_updates",
        "check.pantheon.php_eol.check_php_eol",
    ]


def test_declarations_match_the_spec_table(psh, reset_sc, request):
    reset_sc.config = {"Check": {"pantheon": {"enabled": True}}}
    load_check_package(psh, "pantheon", "pantheon_decl_probe", request)
    hooks = {h["name"]: h
             for phase in ("site_pre", "site_post_gather")
             for h in reset_sc.hooks[phase]}
    assert hooks["check.pantheon.frozen.check_frozen_site"]["consumes"] == []
    assert hooks["check.pantheon.live_env.check_live_env"]["consumes"] == ["envs"]
    assert hooks["check.pantheon.updates.check_upstream_updates"]["consumes"] == []
    assert hooks["check.pantheon.php_eol.check_php_eol"]["consumes"] == ["envs"]
    assert all(h["produces"] == [] for h in hooks.values())


def test_disabled_registers_nothing_and_says_so(psh, reset_sc, request, monkeypatch):
    console = recording_console(monkeypatch, reset_sc)
    reset_sc.config = {"Check": {"pantheon": {"enabled": False}}}
    load_check_package(psh, "pantheon", "pantheon_off_probe", request)
    assert not reset_sc.hooks.get("site_pre")
    assert not reset_sc.hooks.get("site_post_gather")
    assert "Skipping check.pantheon" in console.export_text()
```

## User

Base directory for this skill: /home/node/.claude/plugins/cache/karpathy-skills/andrej-karpathy-skills/1.0.0/skills/karpathy-guidelines

# Karpathy Guidelines

Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes, derived from [Andrej Karpathy's observations](https://x.com/karpathy/status/2015883857489522876) on LLM coding pitfalls.

**Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.

## 1. Think Before Coding

**Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**

Before implementing:
- State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
- If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
- If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
- If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.

## 2. Simplicity First

**Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**

- No features beyond what was asked.
- No abstractions for single-use code.
- No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
- No error handling for impossible scenarios.
- If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.

Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.

## 3. Surgical Changes

**Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**

When editing existing code:
- Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
- Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
- Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
- If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.

When your changes create orphans:
- Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
- Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.

The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.

## 4. Goal-Driven Execution

**Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**

Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
- "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
- "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
- "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"

For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
```
1. [Step] → verify: [check]
2. [Step] → verify: [check]
3. [Step] → verify: [check]
```

Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.

Elements MUST already be strings — the projection does not\ncoerce, so a format spec (`f\"{savings:.2f}\"`, `str(num_updates)`) is the producer's job and\nstays visible at the producer.\n\n**Why not the alternatives** (both rejected in the design round): a `csv_suffix: str` keeps\nthe comma-joining scattered across 22 producers and models nothing; a full `csv: str`\noverride re-admits the free-form string the type exists to retire and hands the site name\nback to producers.\n\n### 2.2 Deliverable B — the projection\n\nToday `SiteContext._notice_to_dict` is private, builds only the two-field csv, and leaves\n`icon`/`text` defaulting to `add_notice`'s fill logic. I14c makes it public and complete:\n\n```python\n    def notice_to_dict(self, notice: Notice) -> dict:\n        \"\"\"Project a Notice onto the render dict this site's report consumes.\"\"\"\n        return {\n            \"type\": str(notice.severity),\n            \"icon\": notice.icon or icon[notice.severity],\n            \"csv\": \",\".join([self[\"site\"][\"name\"], notice.code, *notice.csv_extra]),\n            \"short\": notice.short,\n            \"message\": notice.html,\n            \"text\": notice.text or html_to_text(notice.html),\n        }\n\n    def add_notice(self, notice: Notice) -> None:\n        if not isinstance(notice, Notice):\n            raise TypeError(...)                      # §2.6\n        d = self.notice_to_dict(notice)\n        if notice.order in (\"prepend\", \"first\"):\n            self[\"notices\"].insert(0, d)\n        else:\n            self[\"notices\"].append(d)\n```\n\nThree consequences, each deliberate:\n\n1. **The site name comes from the `SiteContext`, never from the producer.** Verified for all\n   37 producers: every one is called with the site name of the site being processed (the\n   builders that take a `site`/`site_name` parameter — `wp_error`, `drush_error`,\n   `check_wordpress_plugin`, `check_drupal_module`, `build_smell_notices`,\n   `build_php_eol_notice`, `build_plan_recommendation_notice`, the dns/cachecheck/cdn-change\n   builders — are all called with `site[\"name\"]` / the context's site name at every call\n   site). Those parameters stay: the console messages inside the builders use them.\n2. **`order` is no longer stored in the render dict.** Nothing downstream reads it\n   (`add_notice` was its only reader; verified by grep — the other two `order` hits are\n   `add_news_item`'s and the projection's), and no producer sets a non-default order today.\n3. **`icon` and `text` are always present** in the stored dict, with the same values\n   `add_notice`'s fill logic produces today. Key *order* within the dict changes; nothing\n   reads a notice dict positionally or serializes it (templates and consumers are all\n   by-key — verified), so this is unobservable.\n\n### 2.3 Deliverable C — code registration and the test-reset seam\n\nEach producing module registers its codes at import, via a module-level constant:\n\n```python\nNOTICE_FROZEN = registry.register(\"frozen\", description=\"site frozen by Pantheon for inactivity\")\n...\n    site_context.add_notice(Notice(severity=Severity.ALERT, code=NOTICE_FROZEN, ...))\n```\n\nRegistration is **per code, not per producer**: `psh/gather.py` registers `not-installed` and\n`turned-off` **once** each even though the WordPress and Drupal builders both emit them —\nregistering twice is exactly the `DuplicateNoticeCodeError` the registry exists to raise.\n\n**The re-import hazard, and its fix.** `NoticeRegistry.register` is import-time-once\nmetadata, but the test suite loads `check/` modules standalone under fresh probe names\n(`tests/helpers/checkload.py`, and direct `spec_from_file_location` in the cloudflare/dns/\nsitelens/plugin test files) — so a module body re-executes once per test, and the second\n`register()` of the same code would raise. Fix:\n\n```python\nclass NoticeRegistry:\n    def snapshot(self) -> dict[str, str]:   # test seam (tests/conftest.py reset_sc)\n        return dict(self._codes)\n    def restore(self, snapshot: dict[str, str]) -> None:\n        self._codes = dict(snapshot)\n```\n\nand the **autouse** `reset_sc` fixture saves/restores it around every test, alongside the\n`script_context` globals it already deep-copies. This is safe because **no test loads the\nsame check module or package twice within one test function** (verified by AST scan over\n`tests/`: zero functions with more than one `load_check_package`/`load_check_module`/\n`exec_module` call). All 10 call sites (7 in\n`psh/gather.py`, 3 in `check/`) and both test call sites pass it positionally (verified), so\nthis is a rename with no call-site churn beyond the definition.\n\n### 2.5 The two producers that do not go through `add_notice`\n\n1. **`check/umich/annual_billing.py`** publishes the hook-produced key\n   `annual_bill_upcoming`, read by `sort_notices_and_subject` and inserted straight into the\n   render-only `sorted_notices` list (never into `site_context[\"notices\"]`, so it never\n   reaches `-notices.csv` — load-bearing, LEDGER I12). This is why the projection is public.\n2. **`psh/cli.py:no_primary_domain_notice`** returns a notice or `None`; `main()` passes the\n   non-`None` result to `add_notice`. sys.exit(1)` guard is **deleted** — it\n  guards a dict that can no longer arrive, and `Notice.html` is a required constructor\n  argument, so the failure it detected is now a `TypeError` at construction.\n- `add_notices(notices: list[Notice])` is unchanged apart from its docstring/annotation.\n- `Notice.order` keeps its `\"prepend\"`/`\"first\"` semantics in `add_notice` even though no\n  producer uses them today: it is part of the §6 field set and `add_news_item` shows the\n  behavior is live for the sibling collection.\n\n### 2.7 Decisions (D-i14c-1…9, exhaustive)\n\n| # | Decision | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| D-i14c-1 | `csv_extra: tuple[str, ...] = ()`, joined after `site,code` | §2.1; user-selected in the design round over `csv_suffix`/`csv` override |\n| D-i14c-2 | The render dict stays the storage form; only the *producer* dict form is retired | Templates/sort/csv all read it; replacing it would move goldens (§8 bar) |\n| D-i14c-3 | The projection becomes public `SiteContext.notice_to_dict` and does the full normalization | One projection for both the `add_notice` path and the billing produced key (DRY); a constant cannot drift from what was registered |\n| D-i14c-7 | `NoticeRegistry.snapshot()/restore()` + autouse `reset_sc` restore | Standalone module loads in tests re-execute module bodies (§2.3); user-selected over an idempotent `register()`, which would silently allow a genuine duplicate |\n| D-i14c-8 | `wp_error`/`drush_error` parameter `code` → `operation` | PD#11: two different things named `code` in one call after conversion |\n| D-i14c-9 | `add_notice` raises `TypeError` on a non-`Notice`; Spec written: `development/2026-07-24-mod-I14c-notice/SPEC.md`, plus the CAMPAIGN.md §6 amendment and its LEDGER record (landed at spec time so the spec's "§6 as amended" citations resolve — the I14a precedent).

**What I found that shapes the increment** (all measured, not assumed):

- **37 producers / 35 distinct codes** (`not-installed` and `turned-off` each have a WP *and* a Drupal producer inside `psh/gather.py` — registering per-producer would trip `DuplicateNoticeCodeError`).
- **Storage stays the render dict.** Templates read `notice.type|icon|message|text`, `sort_notices_and_subject` reads `["type"]`/`["short"]`, `record_site_notices` reads `["csv"]`. Keeping it means ~24 hook/snapshot test files and all 107 `.ambr` files need **no edit** and keep their evidentiary value; only ~10 builder-level test files repoint to `Notice` attributes.
- **Only ONE notice has a genuinely custom icon** (annual-bill 💵). The other 34 explicit icons equal `sc.icon[type]` — measured by AST, so the conversion deletes 34 unasserted HTML-entity literals. `check_drupal_module`'s hand-rolled level→icon map is a duplicate of `sc.icon` that would ship a *warning triangle on an alert* if anyone passed `level="alert"`; deriving from `Severity(level)` kills it.
- **The registry re-import hazard is real, not theoretical**: tests load `check/` modules standalone once per test, so import-time `register()` needs the `snapshot`/`restore` seam in autouse `reset_sc`. Verified safe: zero test functions load the same module twice.
- **PD#11 collision**: after conversion `wp_error(site, code, …)`'s `code` param (`"version-check"`) sits next to `Notice.code == "wp-error"`. Renaming it to `operation` is free — all 10 call sites and both test call sites are positional.
- **One coverage gap to close**: `sitelens-url-paths` is the only code with zero csv assertion anywhere in the suite.
- Top risk is **Invariant 8** (re-indenting a column-0 `f"""` literal while re-keying it). The spec's instrument I2a is an AST `ast.dump` equality check of the old dict value nodes vs. the new keyword nodes, with a mandatory red demo — mechanical proof rather than `git diff -w`.

```
## Verdict: **APPROVE-WITH-FIXES** — but implementation MUST NOT start until findings 1–8 are folded

The core design (a `csv_extra` tuple, a public `SiteContext.notice_to_dict`, import-time code registration) survives scrutiny: I independently re-derived the 37 producers, the 35 distinct codes, the 20 files, the 107 `.ambr` snapshots, the site-name provenance for every producer, and the "only `add_notice` reads `order`" claim — all four hold. What does **not** survive is the spec's *measured* arithmetic, its re-import hazard analysis, and — most seriously — its test plan and acceptance bar, which are unachievable as written. Several of these are exactly the failure mode this repo has demonstrated before: a number asserted from an artifact's appearance rather than read from the tree.

All paths below are absolute. Spec = `/workspace/development/2026-07-24-mod-I14c-notice/SPEC.md`.

---

### 1. [CRITICAL — MUST FOLD] §5(2) and §3/§8's `.ambr` byte-identity bar are both false; at least 6 snapshot files break and 9 snapshots cannot survive

**Spec location:** §5 kind 2 (lines 342–344): *"**Hook-level and snapshot tests untouched.** They read `site_context["notices"][…]`, which is still a render dict. This is the single biggest reason for D-i14c-2: ~24 test files and all 107 `.ambr` files need no edit and therefore keep their full evidentiary value."* — plus §3 line 289 (*"All 107 `.ambr` snapshots | **Byte-identical.** No exception is claimed."*) and §8 line 385 (`git diff <spec-commit> -- '*.ambr'   # MUST be empty`).

**Evidence.** Several files the spec calls "snapshot tests" do **not** read `site_context["notices"]` — they snapshot the **builder return value directly**:

```
/workspace/tests/integration/test_plan_recommendation_notice_render.py:8-10
    assert psh.build_plan_recommendation_notice(
        "s", "Performance Medium", "Performance Small", 1234.5, 42, umich
    ) == snapshot            # whole dict -> syrupy
```
```
/workspace/tests/integration/test_dns_notice_render.py:22-40
    assert dns_notices.transient_notice("s", HOSTS) == snapshot     # whole dict, x7
```

And the stored snapshots are whole dicts, including `csv`:

```
$ head -5 /workspace/tests/integration/__snapshots__/test_dns_notice_render.ambr
# name: test_multiple_zones_render
  dict({
    'csv': 's,proxied-in-multiple-cloudflare-zones,a.example.org,b.example.org',
```
```
$ for f in $(find tests -name '*.ambr'); do echo "$(grep -c '^  dict({' $f)  $f"; done | sort -rn | head -3
7  tests/integration/__snapshots__/test_dns_notice_render.ambr
2  tests/integration/__snapshots__/test_plan_recommendation_notice_render.ambr
0  tests/integration/__snapshots__/test_wordpress_notice_render.ambr
```

Once those builders return a `Notice`, syrupy serializes a dataclass, not a `dict({…})` — **9 `.ambr` entries change by construction**. Projecting through `notice_to_dict` instead does not rescue byte-identity either: the dns snapshots have **no `'icon'` key** (the builders omit it and the raw return is snapshotted), while §2.2's projection always emits `icon`.

Further files that break and are **not** in §5(1)'s repoint list:

| File | Line | Breaking construct |
|---|---|---|
| `/workspace/tests/integration/test_smell_notice_render.py` | 11–13 | `(n,) = psh.build_smell_notices(...)` then `n["message"]` |
| `/workspace/tests/integration/test_drupal_notice_render.py` | 27–35, 80–88 | `psh.check_drupal_module(...)[0]["csv"]`, `psh.no_primary_domain_notice(...)["message"]` |
| `/workspace/tests/integration/test_wordpress_notice_render.py` | 70–100 | `check_wordpress_plugin(SITE, [], *PAPC_ARGS)` → `n["csv"]`, `n["type"]` |
| `/workspace/tests/integration/test_pantheon_notice_render.py` | 74–78 | `mod.build_php_eol_notice(SITE, version)` → `n["message"]` |
| `/workspace/tests/integration/test_cachecheck_notice_render.py` | 70, 81 | `ctx.add_notice(dict(out[0]))` → `dict(Notice)` raises `TypeError` |
| `/workspace/tests/integration/test_pantheon_cdn_change_notice_render.py` | 52, 62 | `ctx.add_notice(dict(built))` → same |

**Why it matters beyond bookkeeping:** §5(2) is stated as *"the single biggest reason for D-i14c-2"*, and §6 rests on it (*"Tasks 2–5 … each leaves the tree green"*). Task 2 alone (which converts `psh/plans.py:185` and `psh/gather.py`'s `check_wordpress_plugin`/`check_drupal_module`) reddens `test_plan_recommendation_notice_render.py`, `test_smell_notice_render.py`, `test_drupal_notice_render.py` and `test_wordpress_notice_render.py`. Directives: **PD#14 — "*A green check is a claim, not evidence, until it has been shown capable of going red on the condition it guards*"**; the corollary here is the inverse — an acceptance criterion (`git diff -- '*.ambr'` MUST be empty) that *will* go red for a benign reason, in a repo whose Invariant 1 reads *"an existing golden going red is a defect in the increment"*, is a trap that invites exactly the refresh CAMPAIGN.md §9 forbids.

**Fix (all three parts):**
1. Replace §5(2) with an exhaustive, per-file table of snapshot/render tests, split into "reads `site_context["notices"]` — untouched" and "reads a builder return — repointed", derived by scan, not by eye.
2. Decide and state explicitly how the 9 whole-dict snapshots are handled. The byte-identity-preserving option is to have those two tests call `SiteContext(...).add_notice(builder(...))` and snapshot `ctx["notices"][0]` — but that still adds `'icon'` to the dns dicts, so the honest options are (a) rewrite those two tests to field-wise assertions with fresh snapshot names, or (b) sanction a scoped, reviewed `.ambr` diff limited to those 9 entries with the diff pasted in the ledger.
3. Amend §3 and §8 accordingly: `.ambr` byte-identity is achievable for **98 of 107**, not all 107. Keep the e2e golden bar at absolute byte-identity (that one I could not falsify).
```
