### 2. [CRITICAL — MUST FOLD] §2.3's re-import fix is provably insufficient: one test loads a producing module at **collection time**, outside every `reset_sc`

**Spec location:** §2.3 lines 170–176: *"This is safe because **no test loads the same check module or package twice within one test function** (verified by AST scan over `tests/`: zero functions with more than one `load_check_package`/`load_check_module`/`exec_module` call)."*

**Evidence.** The scan's premise is true as a call-site count (I re-ran it and got zero), but it is **structurally blind to module-level loads** — and there is exactly one:

```
$ python3 scan_toplevel.py     # walks only top-level statements, skipping FunctionDef/ClassDef
TOP-LEVEL tests/unit/test_php_eol_notice.py:14  SourceFileLoader('php_eol_for_unit_tests', str(_PATH)).load_module()
```
```
/workspace/tests/unit/test_php_eol_notice.py:13-15
_PATH = Path(psh.__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "check" / "pantheon" / "php_eol.py"
build_php_eol_notice = SourceFileLoader(
    "php_eol_for_unit_tests", str(_PATH)).load_module().build_php_eol_notice
```

`check/pantheon/php_eol.py` produces two codes (`/workspace/check/pantheon/php_eol.py:12` and `:39`), so under D-i14c-6 it registers `php-eol-warning`/`php-eol-alert` at import. That import happens during **collection**, before any `reset_sc` snapshot exists. `snapshot()/restore()` can only undo registrations made *during* a test; it cannot remove one made before the snapshot. Every later load of that module then raises `DuplicateNoticeCodeError`:

- `/workspace/tests/integration/test_check_pantheon.py:74` (`load_check_module(..., "php_eol", "pantheon_phpeol_probe", ...)`)
- `/workspace/tests/integration/test_pantheon_notice_render.py:72` (parametrized, twice)
- `/workspace/tests/integration/test_hook_dag.py:57` loading `("check", "pantheon", …)` — i.e. the roster test **I4 itself** would error.

Two further blind spots in the same instrument: it counts **call sites**, so `test_hook_dag.py:56-57`'s `for base, package, probe in ALL_PACKAGES: load_check_package(...)` reads as *one* load while performing twelve; and it ignores **fixture scope** — `/workspace/tests/unit/test_cachecheck_headers.py:21` is `@pytest.fixture(scope="module")`, and a module-scoped fixture is set up *before* the function-scoped autouse `reset_sc`, so anything it registers also escapes the snapshot. (That one is benign today only because `headers.py` produces no notices — a fact the spec does not rely on because it never considered scope.)

**Fix:** (a) name `/workspace/tests/unit/test_php_eol_notice.py` in §2.3 and require its module-level `SourceFileLoader(...).load_module()` to move into a function-scoped fixture (the `test_annual_billing_notices.py:13` pattern already in the tree); (b) restate the safety condition as *"no producing module is executed outside a function-scoped `reset_sc`, and no single test executes the same producing module twice"*; (c) replace the AST scan with one that also walks module-level statements and reports fixture scope, and show it red against the current `test_php_eol_notice.py` — per PD#14, this instrument must be demonstrated able to catch the case it certifies. (d) Note in §2.3 that `registry.codes()` is **config-gated** (`check/umich/__init__.py` and `check/cloudflare/__init__.py` import their producing submodules only inside the `enabled` guard), so I4's roster test MUST run under `test_hook_dag.py`'s `EVERYTHING_ENABLED` — currently unstated.

### 3. [HIGH — MUST FOLD] §2.4's "the `info` branch is dead" is false; the branch is live *and* test-pinned

**Spec location:** §2.4 lines 231–234: *"The `level` parameter itself stays (public builder signature); no in-tree caller passes it today — both callers take the `"warning"` default, so the `info` branch is dead and dies as an orphan of this change, not as unrelated cleanup."*

**Evidence.** There are **three** callers of `check_drupal_module`, and one passes `level="info"`:

```
/workspace/check/umich/cloudflare_cms.py:28-31
    ("purge_processor_cron",
     "Purge Cron Processor (purge_processor_cron)",
     "Recommended as a fallback for clearing Cloudflare's caches when content is updated.",
     {"level": "info"}),
```
```
/workspace/check/umich/cloudflare_cms.py:57-59
        for slug, display_name, reason, kwargs in DRUPAL_MODULES:
            site_context.add_notices(sc.check_drupal_module(
                site, mods, slug, display_name, DOC_DRUPAL, reason, **kwargs))
```

And it is pinned:

```
/workspace/tests/integration/test_check_umich_cloudflare_cms.py:122-127
    # None of the four U-M Cloudflare modules are installed -> four notices
    # (3 warning + 1 info for purge_processor_cron), all "not-installed".
    assert len(ctx["notices"]) == 4
    assert sorted(n["type"] for n in ctx["notices"]) == ["info", "warning", "warning", "warning"]
```

The other two callers (`/workspace/check/drupal/papc.py:10`, `/workspace/check/drupal/d7_eol.py:36`) do take the default. The *conclusion* of D-i14c-5 still holds — the local map's `info` value equals `sc.icon['info']` and its `warning` value equals `sc.icon['warning']`, so `Severity(level)` is icon-identical — but the spec reaches it through a false premise, and an implementer told "the `info` branch is dead" may delete the path that produces a live production notice for every U-M Drupal site missing `purge_processor_cron`.

**Fix:** correct §2.4 to "three callers; `check/umich/cloudflare_cms.py` passes `level='info'` for `purge_processor_cron`, pinned by `tests/integration/test_check_umich_cloudflare_cms.py:127`. The `info` branch is **live**; `Severity(level)` reproduces both reachable icons exactly, and the `alert` case — which the local map gets wrong — remains unreachable." CAMPAIGN.md §7 obligation (4): *"verify — not assume — every claim in comments/docs it moves or writes."*

### 4. [HIGH — MUST FOLD] §6 leaves producer #14 assigned to no task

**Spec location:** §6 task table, lines 359–362.

**Evidence.** Task 2 = producers 1–13. Task 3 = "Producers 21–28, 36, 37". Task 4 = 15–20, 29. Task 5 = 30–35. Union = {1–13, 15–37} — **#14, `check/addon_updates/table.py:66` (`updates-addons`), is in no task.** The row's own count is also wrong: 21–28 is 8 producers, plus 36 and 37 = **10**, not the stated "11 literals" (the 11th is presumably the orphaned #14, which the numeric range then excludes). Cross-check: 13 + 10 + 7 + 6 = 36 of 37.

Because Task 6 retires the dict form globally, an unconverted #14 becomes a runtime `TypeError` on every site with pending add-on updates — and the `check/addon_updates` tests read the *context* dict, so nothing in §5 would catch it before Task 6.

**Fix:** add `14` to Task 3's producer list and correct the count to 11; add a mechanical close-gate to §8 (see finding 8) that proves zero producers remain.

### 5. [HIGH — MUST FOLD] §5's repoint list is incomplete, and instrument I5 is un-runnable after Deliverable E

**Spec location:** §5(1) lines 334–341; §5 line 331 (*"No test file is deleted."*); §4 instrument I5 line 323; §2.6 line 262.

**Evidence.** These pass **dicts** to `add_notice` and are absent from §5's list:

```
/workspace/tests/unit/test_site_context.py:31,36,40,55,75,83,92,97,98,104   ctx.add_notice(_n(...))  # _n() returns a dict
/workspace/tests/unit/test_add_notice_from_notice.py:17-20                   from_dict.add_notice({...})
/workspace/tests/unit/test_cachecheck_consolidation.py:243,460,466           ctx.add_notice(dict(...))
```

Two specific consequences:

- `/workspace/tests/unit/test_site_context.py:89-92` is `test_add_notice_missing_message_exits`, asserting `pytest.raises(SystemExit)` — it pins **exactly the guard D-i14c-9 deletes**. §5's "No test file is deleted" does not address a *test* that must be deleted or rewritten. State it, with the replacement (`pytest.raises(TypeError)`) named.
- Instrument I5 is described as *"`tests/unit/test_add_notice_from_notice.py` full-dict equality (`Notice`-projected == the pre-change literal)"*. Its current mechanism is `from_notice["notices"] == from_dict["notices"]` (line 21) — which requires feeding `add_notice` a dict. After Deliverable E that is a `TypeError`, so **the instrument as specified cannot run at close**. It must be restated as equality against a frozen expected-dict literal, not against a second `add_notice` call.

**Fix:** extend §5(1) with the three files above; add an explicit §5(4) "tests deleted or semantically rewritten (exhaustive)" listing `test_add_notice_missing_message_exits`; restate I5's mechanism.

### 6. [HIGH — MUST FOLD] D-i14c-5's icon counts are wrong (34/35 → measured 26/29), and contradict the spec's own table

**Spec location:** §2.4 lines 222–225 (*"So the conversion **deletes 34 icon literals**, and with them 34 chances of a silent typo"*) and D-i14c-5 line 278 (*"Measured equal to the default (§4 I2b)"* … "the other 34 derive from severity").

**Evidence.** AST scan over `psh/`, `check/`, `plugin/` of every dict literal carrying a `"csv"` key:

```
--- with icon key: 29   without: 8   constant-and-equal-to-default: 26
MISMATCH:     [check/umich/annual_billing.py:23  type='alert'  icon='&#x1F4B5;']
NON-CONSTANT: [psh/gather.py:157, psh/gather.py:174  icon = <the check_drupal_module local variable>]
```

So: **29** producers carry an explicit `icon`, of which **26** are literals equal to `sc.icon[type]`, 2 are the `check_drupal_module` variable, and 1 is the custom 💵. The conversion removes **28** icon entries (29 − the 1 kept), of which **26** are the HTML-entity literals the prose is counting. The spec's own §2.4 table already says this — 26 rows marked "default", 2 marked "local map", 1 marked "CUSTOM 💵" — so the prose contradicts the table three lines below it. "34/35" appears nowhere in the tree; it looks like the 35-distinct-*codes* figure leaking into an icon sentence.

**Fix:** replace "34"/"35" with "26 literal defaults (+ 2 via `check_drupal_module`'s local map)" everywhere in §2.4 and D-i14c-5, and have instrument I2b print the counts so the spec's numbers come from the scan rather than the other way round.

### 8. [MEDIUM — MUST FOLD] §8's acceptance block is not runnable, and its headline instrument is quote-blind

**Spec location:** §8 lines 382–395.

**Evidence.**

```
$ grep -rn '"csv":' psh/ check/ plugin/ script_context.py | wc -l
37
$ grep -rn "'csv':" psh/ check/ plugin/ script_context.py | wc -l
1        # /workspace/check/umich/sitelens.py:108   'csv': f'{site_name},sitelens-url-paths,...'
```

The spec's close-gate — *"`grep -rn '"csv":' psh/ check/ plugin/ script_context.py` returning **exactly one** hit … (the mechanical proof that no producer still builds a notice dict)"* — **already cannot see producer #35**, because `sitelens.py` uses single quotes. It would return 1 with `sitelens.py` left entirely unconverted. Combined with finding 4 (producer #14 orphaned), that is a real chance of shipping a dict producer past a green gate. This is PD#14 verbatim: *"Your instruments can lie … A test, golden, fixture, shim, counter, log line, or metric is code, and can be silently wrong."*

Also, line 388 reads `python - <<'…'  # instrument I2a over the whole conversion, plus its red demo` — an ellipsis where a heredoc should be. The I2a script is referenced by four tasks but is written nowhere, and §4 says its output "MUST be run and pasted". The Spine's bar: *"Acceptance criteria = exact commands + expected output, **run and pasted**, never summarized."*

**Fix:** (a) change the gate to `grep -rnE "['\"]csv['\"][[:space:]]*:" psh/ check/ plugin/ script_context.py`, expected exactly 1; (b) add a second, quote-independent gate — the AST scan I used above (`ast.Dict` with a `"csv"` key) must return `TOTAL 1`; (c) write the I2a and I2b scripts into the spec (or into a committed `development/2026-07-24-mod-I14c-notice/tools/` file with a resolvable path), since a fresh-context implementer cannot ask.

### 9. [MEDIUM — should fold] Roster/producer counts are internally inconsistent (`no-domains` is a 38th producer and a 36th code)

**Spec location:** Glossary lines 14–16 (*"**Producer** — a place in `psh/` or `check/` that constructs one notice. There are **37** (§2.4 table, exhaustive)"*); §1.1 row C (*"Notice-code registration for all 35 codes"*); §4 I4 (*"assert `registry.codes()` equals the 35-code roster + `no-domains`"*).

**Evidence.** `/workspace/psh/cli.py:662-679` constructs a notice (`Notice(severity=Severity.ALERT, code="no-domains", …)`), and `/workspace/psh/cli.py:140` already registers it. Under the glossary's own definition that is a 38th producer and a 36th code — so "§2.4 table, exhaustive" is exhaustive of *dict-form* producers only, and Deliverable C's "35 codes" understates the roster by one. PD#11 (*"Terminology stays clear and consistent — within the new design and across the existing codebase"*) and the Spine's *"Every list marked exhaustive or illustrative."*

**Fix:** define **Producer** as "a place that hands `add_notice` a hand-built **dict**" (37, exhaustive), add a one-line note that `no-domains` (`psh/cli.py:665`) is the 38th notice site, already `Notice`-based since I3, and state the roster as **36** codes throughout (§1.1 C, D-i14c-6, I4).

---

### 10. [MEDIUM — should fold] §2.1's byte-identity claim has an unstated empty-list precondition (PD#3)

**Spec location:** §2.1 lines 77–87, headed *"Every current csv shape reproduces byte-for-byte."*

**Evidence.** For the six `",".join(hostnames)` / `",".join(f.fqdn …)` producers, today's expression is `f"{site},{code}," + ",".join(xs)`. With `xs == []` that yields a **trailing comma**; `",".join([site, code, *()])` yields **no trailing comma**. Byte-identity therefore holds only because every call site is guarded:

```
/workspace/check/dns/hook.py:26,30,33,36,40   if site_context["dns_transient"]: … if site_context["not_in_dns"]: …
/workspace/check/pantheon_cdn_change/hook.py:50-51   if not findings: return
```

The builders are **pure and unit-tested directly** (`tests/unit/test_dns_notices.py`, `tests/unit/test_pantheon_cdn_change_notices.py`), so a future test or caller passing `[]` silently changes a csv row that today ends in a comma. PD#3: *"Every flow has a happy path plus three shadows: nil input, empty/zero-length input, and upstream error. Trace all four for every new flow."*

**Fix:** state the precondition in §2.1 ("`csv_extra=tuple(xs)` is byte-identical **iff `xs` is non-empty**; every current call site guards this — `check/dns/hook.py:26/30/33/36/40`, `check/pantheon_cdn_change/hook.py:50`"), and add one unit test per shape asserting the empty case explicitly so the divergence is pinned rather than latent.

---

### 11. [LOW-MEDIUM — should fold] §2.2 consequence 3 and §3's "no console line is touched" are both false at `-v`

**Spec location:** §2.2 line 139–141 (*"nothing reads a notice dict positionally or serializes it (templates and consumers are all by-key — verified), so this is unobservable"*); §3 line 292 (*"stdout / console | Unchanged in practice (no console line is touched)"*).

**Evidence.**
```
/workspace/psh/cli.py:879
            sc.debug("===== Notices:\n", site_context["notices"])
```
`sc.debug` → `console.log(...)`, which pretty-prints the dicts in **insertion order**. For the 8 producers that omit `icon` today (`check/dns/notices.py` ×5, `check/cloudflare/notices.py:397`, `check/pantheon_cdn_change/notices.py:197`, `check/umich/sitelens.py:106`) the stored order is currently `type, csv, short, message, text, icon` (icon appended by the fill logic at `/workspace/script_context.py:126-127`); the new projection emits `type, icon, csv, short, message, text`. That output *does* change at `-v`. It is sanctioned by CAMPAIGN.md §8 ("stdout / console / error messages MAY improve freely"), so this is a wording defect, not a bar violation — but PD#5 makes observability first-class and the claim as written is simply not true.

**Fix:** replace "unobservable" with "observable only in the `-v` `sc.debug("===== Notices:…")` dump (`psh/cli.py:879`), where key order for the 8 icon-less producers changes; sanctioned by CAMPAIGN.md §8", and soften §3's console row to "MAY change (key order in the `-v` notices dump); no bar surface affected".

---

### 12. [LOW-MEDIUM — should fold] Instrument I2b is a one-shot measurement, not a regression instrument — and nothing permanently pins severity→icon

**Spec location:** §4 instrument I2b, line 320.

**Evidence.** I2b scans **today's** literals. After Task 2–5 those literals are gone, so I2b can never go red again; its stated red demo (*"Change one expected icon in the scratch script and show it reporting a mismatch"*) tests the scratch script, not the program. Post-conversion the icon is produced entirely by `notice_to_dict`, and the `.ambr` snapshots assert only `message`/`text`/`short` — I checked: no `.ambr` contains an `icon` value, and only `/workspace/tests/integration/test_check_pantheon_cdn_change.py:57` asserts one. So for the 26 formerly-explicit icons, the only evidence they still render correctly is a scan that ran once.

**Fix:** add a permanent unit test to §5(3) asserting `notice_to_dict` yields `sc.icon['info'|'warning'|'alert']` for each `Severity`, plus the 💵 override — a three-line test that *can* go red, per PD#14.

---

### 13. [LOW — optional] `wp_error`/`drush_error` call-site count is 12, not 10

**Spec location:** §2.4 line 241: *"All 10 call sites (7 in `psh/gather.py`, 3 in `check/`)…"*

**Evidence.** `psh/gather.py`: 203, 232, 261, 310, 358, 382, 403 = **7** ✓. `check/`: `wordpress/ocp.py:28`, `wordpress/favicon.py:24`, `drupal/multisite.py:29`, `umich/drupal_ua.py:49`, `umich/drupal_ua.py:64` = **5**, not 3. Total **12**. The substantive part of the claim — all pass the parameter positionally, so D-i14c-8's rename costs no call-site churn — I verified and it holds (including both test sites, `tests/integration/test_wrappers.py:85,96`).

---

### 14. [LOW — optional] No diagram (PD#8)

PD#8: *"No non-trivial flow ships undiagrammed in the spec — ASCII art for every new data flow, state machine, processing pipeline, dependency graph, and decision tree."* I14c introduces a cross-file data flow (producer → `Notice` → `notice_to_dict` → render dict → template / `record_site_notices` → `-notices.csv`, with the `annual_bill_upcoming` key bypassing `site_context["notices"]`) and a lifecycle (import → `registry.register` → `reset_sc` snapshot/restore per test). Both are non-local and both are where the increment's two hardest bugs live (findings 1 and 2). Ten lines of ASCII would have made the `annual_bill_upcoming` bypass and the registration-vs-snapshot ordering visible at a glance.

---

## What verified clean (so the next round doesn't re-litigate it)

- **37 dict-form producers / 35 distinct codes / 20 files** — re-derived by AST scan; every `file:line` in the §2.4 table matches the dict literal's `lineno` exactly, and every severity, csv shape, and own-vs-derived-`text` cell is correct. The table is exhaustive of dict-form producers (no notice is built by `dict()`, `.update()`, or a copy; scan for `message`/`short` dicts lacking `csv` found nothing in `psh/`/`check/`/`plugin/`).
- **§2.2 consequence 1 (site-name provenance)** — every builder call site passes the context's site name: `psh/gather.py` ×7 (`site["name"]`), `check/wordpress/ocp.py:29`, `check/wordpress/favicon.py:25`, `check/drupal/multisite.py:30`, `check/umich/drupal_ua.py:50,65`, `check/umich/cloudflare_cms.py:36` (`site = site_context["site"]["name"]` — reads like a dict but is the name), `check/cloudflare/cache.py:276`, `check/pantheon_cdn_change/hook.py:37`, `check/pantheon/php_eol.py:75`, `check/umich/sitelens.py:88`, `psh/cli.py:874`. No mismatch.
- **D-i14c-4 (`order`)** — `grep -rn "'order'\|\"order\"" psh/ check/ plugin/ script_context.py tests/` returns exactly the three hits the spec predicts (`script_context.py:130`, `:152`, `:197`), plus one comment in `test_add_notice_from_notice.py:21`. Nothing downstream reads it; templates don't.
- **§4's `sitelens-url-paths` gap** — confirmed: it is the only one of the 35 codes with zero occurrences anywhere in `tests/`.
- **107 `.ambr` snapshots** across 15 files — count is right.
- **§2.1's awkward shapes** — `no-primary-domain,` → `csv_extra=("",)`, the `\,`-escaped `json.dumps` payloads, the `'+'.join` fields, and `f"{savings:.2f}"` all reproduce exactly under `",".join([site, code, *csv_extra])` for non-empty inputs. I found no shape that cannot be expressed.
- **CAMPAIGN.md §6 amendment and its LEDGER record** are genuinely present at `982589f` (apart from the "22" in finding 7).

## Directives applied

- **PD#14** — *"Your instruments can lie. A test, golden, fixture, shim, counter, log line, or metric is code, and can be silently wrong. **A green check is a claim, not evidence, until it has been shown capable of going red on the condition it guards.**"* → findings 1, 2, 8, 12.
- **PD#3** — *"Every flow has a happy path plus three shadows: nil input, empty/zero-length input, and upstream error. Trace all four for every new flow."* → finding 10.
- **PD#5** — *"Observability is scope, not an afterthought."* → finding 11.
- **PD#8** — *"No non-trivial flow ships undiagrammed in the spec — ASCII art for every new data flow, state machine, processing pipeline, dependency graph, and decision tree."* → finding 14.
- **PD#11** — *"Terminology stays clear and consistent — within the new design and across the existing codebase."* → finding 9.
- **Spine, spec quality bar** — *"Every list marked exhaustive or illustrative"* → findings 4, 5, 9; *"Acceptance criteria = exact commands + expected output, **run and pasted**, never summarized. Run them before submitting: an unrun acceptance suite is PD#14 exactly."* → finding 8; *"Seams under test are named and agreed — in the spec, before any implementation."* → finding 1 (the builder-return seam used by six render tests is never named).
- **CAMPAIGN.md §7 obligation (4)** — *"verify — not assume — every claim in comments/docs it moves or writes."* → findings 3, 6, 7, 13.
- **CAMPAIGN.md §9 Invariant 1** — *"Four e2e goldens byte-identical … refresh is forbidden — an existing golden going red is a defect in the increment, PD#14."* → finding 1 (the `.ambr` bar must not be conflated with this one).

No finding here repeats a previous round — this is round 1 for this spec (`982589f` is its first commit).

{
  "file_path": "/workspace/development/2026-07-24-mod-I14c-notice/tools/literal_equality.py",
  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env python\n\"\"\"I14c instrument I2a: prove that converting a notice dict to a Notice(...) call left every\nnotice literal BYTE-IDENTICAL (CAMPAIGN.md section 9, Invariant 8).\n\n    python development/2026-07-24-mod-I14c-notice/tools/literal_equality.py <baseline-rev> [path ...]\n    python development/2026-07-24-mod-I14c-notice/tools/literal_equality.py --self-test <baseline-rev> <path>\n\nFor each file it collects the notice-body literal NODES on both sides -- baseline: the\n\"message\"/\"text\"/\"short\" values of every dict literal carrying a \"csv\" key; working tree: the\nsame, plus the html=/text=/short= keyword arguments of every Notice(...) call -- and compares\nthe two multisets of `ast.dump(node)`.\n\nWhy ast.dump of the node, and not `git diff -w` or an eyeball: an f-string's Constant segments\ncarry the literal's interior whitespace exactly, including the column-0 continuation lines this\nrepo's notice bodies deliberately use.  `git diff -w` is designed to hide precisely the change\nthat would break them (CLAUDE.md, \"git diff -w is not proof\"), and a re-indent that shifts a\nnotice body adds leading whitespace to a rendered email.  Comparing the parsed nodes catches it\nmechanically.  Field RENAMES are invisible to this check by design (message -> html is a key\nchange, not a literal change), which is exactly what makes it a conversion instrument.\n\n--self-test re-indents one baseline literal IN MEMORY (never on disk) and asserts the check\nreports it: PD#14 -- \"a green check is a claim, not evidence, until it has been shown capable\nof going red on the condition it guards\".\n\"\"\"\nimport argparse\nimport ast\nimport collections\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\n\nBODY_KEYS = (\"message\", \"text\", \"short\")\nBODY_KWARGS = (\"html\", \"text\", \"short\")\n\n\ndef literal_nodes(source: str) -> list[ast.expr]:\n    \"\"\"Every notice-body literal node in `source`, dict form and Notice form alike.\"\"\"\n    nodes: list[ast.expr] = []\n    tree = ast.parse(source)\n    for node in ast.walk(tree):\n        if isinstance(node, ast.Dict):\n            pairs = {k.value: v for k, v in zip(node.keys, node.values, strict=False)\n                     if isinstance(k, ast.Constant) and isinstance(k.value, str)}\n            if \"csv\" in pairs:\n                nodes.extend(pairs[key] for key in BODY_KEYS if key in pairs)\n        elif isinstance(node, ast.Call) and getattr(node.func, \"id\", None) == \"Notice\":\n            nodes.extend(kw.value for kw in node.keywords if kw.arg in BODY_KWARGS)\n    return nodes\n\n\ndef dumps(source: str) -> collections.Counter:\n    return collections.Counter(ast.dump(n) for n in literal_nodes(source))\n\n\ndef baseline_source(rev: str, path: str) -> str:\n    return subprocess.run([\"git\", \"show\", f\"{rev}:{path}\"],  # noqa: S603, S607\n                          capture_output=True, check=True, text=True).stdout\n\n\ndef reindent_first_literal(source: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Mutate one literal's interior whitespace, for --self-test.  In memory only.\"\"\"\n    nodes = literal_nodes(source)\n    for node in nodes:\n        if isinstance(node, ast.JoinedStr):\n            for part in node.values:\n                if isinstance(part, ast.Constant) and \"\\n\" in str(part.value):\n                    part.value = str(part.value).replace(\"\\n\", \"\\n \", 1)\n                    return ast.unparse(ast.parse(source))  # never used: see below\n    raise SystemExit(\"self-test: no multi-line literal found in this file\")\n\n\ndef compare(rev: str, paths: list[str]) -> int:\n    failures = 0\n    for path in paths:\n        before, after = dumps(baseline_source(rev, path)), dumps(open(path).read())  # noqa: PTH123, SIM115\n        only_before, only_after = before - after, after - before\n        if only_before or only_after:\n            failures += 1\n            print(f\"CHANGED  {path}\")\n            for dump in only_before:\n                print(f\"    only in {rev}: {dump[:160]}...\")\n            for dump in only_after:\n                print(f\"    only in working tree: {dump[:160]}...\")\n        else:\n            print(f\"identical  {path}  ({sum(before.values())} notice literals)\")\n    print(f\"\\n{len(paths) - failures}/{len(paths)} files with byte-identical notice literals\")\n    return 1 if failures else 0\n\n\ndef self_test(rev: str, path: str) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Show the comparison going RED on a one-space re-indent of a real literal.\"\"\"\n    source = baseline_source(rev, path)\n    before = dumps(source)\n    tree = ast.parse(source)\n    mutated = None\n    for node in ast.walk(tree):\n        if isinstance(node, ast.JoinedStr):\n            for part in node.values:\n                if isinstance(part, ast.Constant) and \"\\n\" in str(part.value):\n                    part.value = str(part.value).replace(\"\\n\", \"\\n \", 1)\n                    mutated = node\n                    break\n        if mutated:\n            break\n    if mutated is None:\n        print(f\"self-test: {path} has no multi-line notice literal to mutate\")\n        return 1\n    after = collections.Counter(ast.dump(n) for n in literal_nodes(ast.unparse(tree)))\n    if before == after:\n        print(f\"SELF-TEST FAILED: a re-indented literal in {path} compared EQUAL -- \"\n              \"this instrument cannot detect the thing it exists to detect\")\n        return 1\n    print(f\"SELF-TEST PASSED: re-indenting one literal in {path} makes the comparison report \"\n          f\"{len((before - after) + (after - before))} changed literal(s) -- the check can go red\")\n    return 0\n\n\ndef main() -> int:\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)\n    parser.add_argument(\"--self-test\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"prove the check can go red\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"rev\", help=\"baseline git revision (e.g. the increment's start commit)\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"paths\", nargs=\"+\", help=\"files to compare\")\n    args = parser.parse_args()\n    if args.self_test:\n        return self_test(args.rev, args.paths[0])\n    return compare(args.rev, args.paths)\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    sys.exit(main())\n"
}

  "content": "# SPEC — I14c: retiring the `Notice` dict form\n\n**Increment:** I14c (Wave 4, third of four). **Date:** 2026-07-24.\n**Governing documents** (read in full before implementing; this spec cites them by section\nand re-derives nothing): `development/2026-07-17-modularization-campaign/CAMPAIGN.md`\n(frozen architecture), `LEDGER.md` (through the I14b entry and the `§6 Notice csv field set`\namendment appended at this spec's time), `/workspace/CLAUDE.md`,\n`/workspace/prompts/directives.md` (the Spine; PD#n citations are to it).\n\n**CAMPAIGN.md §11 row I14c, verbatim:** \"`Notice` dict form retired: the reserved §6\ncsv-field amendment + every producer converted; artifacts byte-identical.\"\n\n**Review record.** Adversarial spec review (fresh context, `psh-reviewer`) round 1 against\ncommit `982589f`: **APPROVE-WITH-FIXES**, 14 findings. All 14 are folded into this revision —\nthe corrections to the measured figures (§2.1, §2.4), the snapshot-impact analysis (§3), the\nregistry hazard's real escape (§2.3), the orphaned producer #14 (§6), the incomplete test\ninventory (§5), the quote-blind close gate (§8), the two diagrams (below), and the two\ninstruments now written as runnable tools (§4). Every figure in this document is now produced\nby `tools/notice_inventory.py`, not asserted.\n\n## Glossary (this spec only; domain terms live in `CONTEXT.md`)\n\n- **Producer** — a place in `psh/`, `check/` or `plugin/` that hands `add_notice` (or a\n  hook-produced key) a **hand-built dict**. There are **37**, exhaustively listed in §2.4 and\n  reproduced by `python development/2026-07-24-mod-I14c-notice/tools/notice_inventory.py`.\n  They carry **35 distinct notice codes** (`not-installed` and `turned-off` each have a\n  WordPress and a Drupal producer inside `psh/gather.py`).\n- **Notice site** — any place a notice is constructed, dict-form or not. There are **38**: the\n  37 producers plus `psh/cli.py:663`'s `no-domains`, which has been `Notice`-based since I3\n  and is **not** converted by this increment. The **roster** is therefore **36 codes**.\n- **Render dict** — the six-key `{type, icon, csv, short, message, text}` mapping stored in\n  `site_context[\"notices\"]`, read by `email_template.{html,txt}`\n  (`notice.type|icon|message|text`), by `sort_notices_and_subject` (`[\"type\"]`, `[\"short\"]`)\n  and by `RunState.record_site_notices` (`[\"csv\"]`). **This form is NOT retired** (D-i14c-2).\n- **Dict form** — a producer building a render dict by hand. This is what I14c retires.\n- **Projection** — `SiteContext.notice_to_dict(notice)`: the one function turning a `Notice`\n  into a render dict (§2.2).\n\nMUST / NEVER / SHOULD / MAY per CAMPAIGN.md §Glossary.\n\n## 0. The two flows this increment changes (PD#8)\n\nNotice construction and consumption, before → after. `*` marks what I14c changes:\n\n```\n  BEFORE                                          AFTER\n  producer builds render dict ──┐                 producer builds Notice ──┐          *\n   {type,icon,csv,short,         │                  (severity, code,        │\n    message,text}                │                   csv_extra, html, …)    │\n                                 ▼                                          ▼\n                    SiteContext.add_notice                     SiteContext.add_notice     *\n                     · fills icon from type                     · TypeError unless Notice\n                     · fills text via html2text                 · notice_to_dict(notice)  *\n                     · order → front/back                         (icon/text/csv filled)\n                                 │                                 · order → front/back\n                                 ▼                                          ▼\n                    site_context[\"notices\"]  ── render dict list (UNCHANGED shape) ──┐\n                                                                                     │\n        ┌────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n        ▼                            ▼                              ▼\n  email_template.{html,txt}   sort_notices_and_subject     RunState.record_site_notices\n  notice.type|icon|           [\"type\"], [\"short\"]          [\"csv\"] → {ymd}-notices.csv\n  message|text                      ▲\n                                    │  annual_bill_upcoming (hook-produced key):\n                                    │  check/umich/annual_billing.py publishes\n                                    └─ site_context.notice_to_dict(Notice)  ── never  *\n                                       enters site_context[\"notices\"], so never\n                                       reaches -notices.csv (load-bearing, LEDGER I12)\n```\n\nCode registration and its test-time lifecycle (§2.3):\n\n```\n  import time (once per process in production; once per LOAD in tests)\n        module body:  NOTICE_FROZEN = registry.register(\"frozen\", description=…)\n                                              │\n                      second registration of the same code ──► DuplicateNoticeCodeError (fatal, loud)\n                                              │\n  ── tests ─────────────────────────────────  ▼  ────────────────────────────────────────\n  reset_sc (autouse, FUNCTION scope)   snapshot() ──► test body loads check module ──► restore()\n        ▲                                                                                │\n        └──── the invariant that makes this work: every producing module is executed ─────┘\n              INSIDE a function-scoped fixture or test body, never at module import\n              of a test file and never in a module/session-scoped fixture.\n```\n\n## 1. Scope\n\n### 1.1 In scope (exhaustive)\n\n| # | Deliverable | Where |\n|---|---|---|\n| A | `Notice` gains `csv_extra: tuple[str, ...]` — the reserved CAMPAIGN.md §6 field-set amendment (deferred I3 → I7 → I10 → I12 → here) | `psh/notice.py` |\n| B | The projection made public and complete: `SiteContext.notice_to_dict` | `script_context.py` |\n| C | Import-time registration of all **36** roster codes (35 converted + `no-domains`, already registered) + the registry test-reset seam | producers, `psh/notice.py`, `tests/conftest.py` |\n| D | All **37** producers converted to construct `Notice` | 20 files (§2.4) |\n| E | The dict form retired: `add_notice` accepts **only** a `Notice` | `script_context.py` |\n| F | Docs: CLAUDE.md notice sections, the CAMPAIGN.md §6 amendment (landed at spec time) + its correction (§2.1), ledger entry, memory | docs |\n\n### 1.2 NOT in scope (reasoning preserved so it is not re-litigated)\n\n- **News items** (`sc.add_news_item`, `sc.news`, the `[News.*]` TOML tables). News items are\n  operator-authored data read from config, not code-built notices; they have no `csv`, no\n  code, and no registry. `add_news_item` keeps its dict path unchanged.\n- **`sections` / `attachments` dicts** — different shapes, different consumers, unrelated to §6.\n- **Notice content, csv values, severities, ordering, or which notices exist.** I14c changes\n  *representation only* (§3).\n- **The render dict itself.** Replacing it with an object (so templates read attributes) would\n  touch `email_template.{html,txt}` and every golden — outside CAMPAIGN.md §8's \"rendered\n  emails NEVER change\" bar, and buying nothing this increment needs.\n- **`no-domains`** (`psh/cli.py:663`) — already `Notice`-based since I3; untouched except that\n  its `registry.register` call at `psh/cli.py:140` joins the roster test.\n- **The three post-campaign README TODOs** (ruff upgrade + PLR0917, typed `sc` stubs + pyright\n  widening, test repoint off the `psh.<name>` surface) — LEDGER I14b says explicitly they are\n  not I14c/I14d scope.\n- **`docs/config-migration.md`, the docs/README/CLAUDE.md wholesale refresh, the §17 closing\n  audit** — I14d.\n\n## 2. Design\n\n### 2.1 Deliverable A — the `csv_extra` field (CAMPAIGN.md §6 amendment)\n\n```python\n@dataclasses.dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Notice:\n    severity: Severity\n    code: str\n    html: str\n    short: str = \"\"\n    text: str = \"\"\n    icon: str = \"\"\n    order: str = \"append\"\n    csv_extra: tuple[str, ...] = ()   # NEW (I14c)\n```\n\nThe projection builds the csv row as `\",\".join([site_name, code, *csv_extra])`. **28 of the\n37** producers carry extra csv fields; 9 are the plain two-field form (measured by\n`tools/notice_inventory.py`; the LEDGER amendment's \"22 of the 37\" is corrected to 28 — see\n§7). Every current shape reproduces byte-for-byte:\n\n| Current literal | `code` | `csv_extra` |\n|---|---|---|\n| `f\"{site['name']},frozen\"` | `\"frozen\"` | `()` |\n| `f\"{site['name']},no-primary-domain,\"` | `\"no-primary-domain\"` | `(\"\",)` — the trailing empty field is real and preserved |\n| `f\"{site},wp-error,{operation},{json.dumps(errors).replace(',', '\\\\,')}\"` | `\"wp-error\"` | `(operation, json.dumps(errors).replace(\",\", \"\\\\,\"))` |\n| `f\"{site_name},not-in-dns,\" + \",\".join(hostnames)` | `\"not-in-dns\"` | `tuple(hostnames)` — see the precondition below |\n| `f\"{site_name},cloudflare-cache,{'+'.join(fqdns)},{'+'.join(ids)}\"` | `\"cloudflare-cache\"` | `(\"+\".join(fqdns), \"+\".join(ids))` |\n| `f\"{site_name},its-recommends-plan,{current_plan},{recommended_plan},{savings:.2f}\"` | `\"its-recommends-plan\"` | `(current_plan, recommended_plan, f\"{savings:.2f}\")` |\n\n`csv_extra` is a **tuple**, not a list: `Notice` is `frozen=True` and a list field would make\nit unhashable and mutably shared. Elements MUST already be strings — the projection does not\ncoerce, so a format spec (`f\"{savings:.2f}\"`, `str(num_updates)`) is the producer's job and\nstays visible at the producer.\n\n**Empty-input precondition (PD#3's zero-length shadow).** For the six join-shaped producers\n(`check/dns/notices.py` ×5, `check/pantheon_cdn_change/notices.py`) today's expression is\n`f\"{site},{code},\" + \",\".join(xs)`, which yields a **trailing comma** when `xs == []`;\n`csv_extra=tuple(xs)` yields no trailing comma. The two forms are therefore byte-identical\n**iff `xs` is non-empty**, which every call site guarantees today\n(`check/dns/hook.py:26,30,33,36,40` each gate on a non-empty list; `check/pantheon_cdn_change/\nhook.py:50` returns early on `not findings`). MUST: state the precondition in each builder's\ndocstring and add one unit test per shape pinning the empty-input result, so the divergence is\n**pinned rather than latent** — these builders are pure and directly unit-tested, so a future\ncaller could reach the empty case. The alternative (`csv_extra=(\",\".join(xs),)`, byte-identical\neven when empty) is rejected: it models the whole hostname list as one opaque field, hiding the\nstructure the csv row actually has, to protect a branch no caller reaches.\n\n**Why not the other shapes** (both rejected in the design round): a `csv_suffix: str` keeps the\ncomma-joining scattered across 28 producers and models nothing; a full `csv: str` override\nre-admits the free-form string the type exists to retire and hands the site name back to\nproducers.\n\n### 2.2 Deliverable B — the projection\n\nToday `SiteContext._notice_to_dict` is private, builds only the two-field csv, and leaves\n`icon`/`text` to `add_notice`'s fill logic. I14c makes it public and complete:\n\n```python\n    def notice_to_dict(self, notice: Notice) -> dict:\n        \"\"\"Project a Notice onto the render dict this site's report consumes.\"\"\"\n        return {\n            \"type\": str(notice.severity),\n            \"icon\": notice.icon or icon[notice.severity],\n            \"csv\": \",\".join([self[\"site\"][\"name\"], notice.code, *notice.csv_extra]),\n            \"short\": notice.short,\n            \"message\": notice.html,\n            \"text\": notice.text or html_to_text(notice.html),\n        }\n\n    def add_notice(self, notice: Notice) -> None:\n        if not isinstance(notice, Notice):\n            raise TypeError(...)                      # §2.6\n        d = self.notice_to_dict(notice)\n        if notice.order in (\"prepend\", \"first\"):\n            self[\"notices\"].insert(0, d)\n        else:\n            self[\"notices\"].append(d)\n```\n\nThree consequences, each deliberate:\n\n1. **The site name comes from the `SiteContext`, never from the producer.** Verified for all\n   37 producers (spec review re-verified independently): every builder that takes a\n   `site`/`site_name` parameter is called with the processed site's name — `psh/gather.py` ×7,\n   `check/wordpress/ocp.py:29`, `check/wordpress/favicon.py:25`, `check/drupal/multisite.py:30`,\n   `check/umich/drupal_ua.py:50,65`, `check/umich/cloudflare_cms.py:36`,\n   `check/cloudflare/cache.py:276`, `check/pantheon_cdn_change/hook.py:37`,\n   `check/pantheon/php_eol.py:75`, `check/umich/sitelens.py:88`, `psh/cli.py:874`. Those\n   parameters stay: the builders' console messages use them.\n2. **`order` is no longer stored in the render dict.** `add_notice` was its only reader (the\n   other two `'order'` hits in the tree are `add_news_item`'s and the projection's), and no\n   producer sets a non-default order today.\n3. **`icon` and `text` are always present**, with the same values `add_notice` fills today. Key\n   *order* within the dict changes. No consumer reads a notice dict positionally or serializes\n   it, with **one observable exception**: `psh/cli.py:879`'s `sc.debug(\"===== Notices:\\n\",\n   site_context[\"notices\"])` pretty-prints the dicts at `-v`, in insertion order — so for the 8\n   producers that omit `icon` today (5 in `check/dns/notices.py`, plus\n   `check/cloudflare/notices.py:397`, `check/pantheon_cdn_change/notices.py:197`,\n   `check/umich/sitelens.py:106`) that debug dump's key order changes. Sanctioned by\n   CAMPAIGN.md §8 (\"stdout / console / error messages MAY improve freely\"); named here rather\n   than claimed absent (PD#5).\n\n### 2.3 Deliverable C — code registration and the test-reset seam\n\nEach producing module registers its codes at import, through a module-level constant:\n\n```python\nNOTICE_FROZEN = registry.register(\"frozen\", description=\"site frozen by Pantheon for inactivity\")\n...\n    site_context.add_notice(Notice(severity=Severity.ALERT, code=NOTICE_FROZEN, ...))\n```\n\nA constant, not a bare `register(...)` call plus a literal at the construction site, so the\ncode used **cannot** drift from the code registered (PD#1). Registration is **per code, not per\nproducer**: `psh/gather.py` registers `not-installed` and `turned-off` **once** each even though\nits WordPress and Drupal builders both emit them — registering twice is exactly the\n`DuplicateNoticeCodeError` the registry exists to raise.\n\n**The re-import hazard.** `NoticeRegistry.register` is import-time-once metadata, but the test\nsuite loads `check/` modules standalone under fresh probe names (`tests/helpers/checkload.py`,\nand direct `SourceFileLoader`/`spec_from_file_location` in the cloudflare/dns/sitelens/plugin/\nphp_eol test files) — so a module body re-executes once per load and the second `register()` of\na given code raises. Fix, in three parts:\n\n```python\nclass NoticeRegistry:\n    def snapshot(self) -> dict[str, str]:   # test seam (tests/conftest.py reset_sc)\n        return dict(self._codes)\n    def restore(self, snapshot: dict[str, str]) -> None:\n        self._codes = dict(snapshot)\n```\n\n1. The **autouse, function-scoped** `reset_sc` fixture saves/restores the registry around every\n   test, alongside the `script_context` globals it already deep-copies.\n2. **The invariant this depends on** (restated correctly after spec-review finding 2, whose\n   original wording — \"no test loads the same module twice in one function\" — was blind to\n   loads that happen *outside* a test): **no producing module may be executed outside a\n   function-scoped fixture or test body.** A load at a test module's import time, or in a\n   module/session-scoped fixture, happens **before** `reset_sc`'s snapshot, so `restore()`\n   cannot undo it and the next load of that module raises. There is exactly **one** violation\n   today, and Task 3 MUST fix it: `tests/unit/test_php_eol_notice.py:13-15` loads\n   `check/pantheon/php_eol.py` (2 codes) at module level via `SourceFileLoader(...).load_module()`\n   — move it into a function-scoped fixture, the pattern `tests/unit/test_annual_billing_notices.py:12`\n   already uses. Without that fix, `tests/integration/test_check_pantheon.py:74`,\n   `test_pantheon_notice_render.py:72` and the roster test itself would all error.\n   The one module-scoped loader fixture in the suite (`tests/unit/test_cachecheck_headers.py:21`)\n   loads `check/cloudflare/headers.py`, which produces no notices — it stays as is, and the\n   rule above is what keeps that fact load-bearing rather than lucky.\n3. **This hazard is fail-loud, not silent** (`DuplicateNoticeCodeError` at collection or test\n   time, naming the code), so no permanent guard test is specified beyond the roster test —\n   PD#1 is satisfied by the error itself.\n\n**Roster test gating.** `check/umich/` and `check/cloudflare/` import their producing submodules\nonly inside their `enabled` guards, so the roster test (§4 I4) MUST load every package with\neverything enabled — `tests/integration/test_hook_dag.py`'s existing all-enabled config and its\n`ALL_PACKAGES` list are the mechanism.\n\nImport-time registration is what makes the duplicate-code guard real: it is the bug class I1\nfixed by hand twice (`php-eol`, `annual-bill`; CAMPAIGN.md §10) and the reason `NoticeRegistry`\nwas built at I3. It also answers §17 Q4 for this façade surface.\n\n### 2.4 Deliverable D — the 37 producers (exhaustive)\n\nReproduce this table with `python development/2026-07-24-mod-I14c-notice/tools/notice_inventory.py`.\n`icon` column: **default** = explicit and equal to `sc.icon[type]` (deleted by the conversion);\n**derived** = no `icon` key today; **local map** = `check_drupal_module`'s variable;\n**CUSTOM** = kept. `text`: own = the literal supplies it; derived = html2text fills it.\n\n| # | Producer | severity | csv today | icon | text |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | `psh/cli.py:301` | info | `{site},no-primary-domain,` | default | own |\n| 2 | `psh/gateway.py:218` | alert | `{site},wp-error,{operation},{json}` | default | own |\n| 3 | `psh/gateway.py:300` | alert | `{site},drush-error,{operation},{json}` | default | own |\n| 4 | `psh/gather.py:86` | warning | `{site},not-installed,{name}` | default | own |\n| 5 | `psh/gather.py:102` | info | `{site},multiple-installed,{name}` | default | own |\n| 6 | `psh/gather.py:122` | warning | `{site},turned-off,{name}` | default | own |\n| 7 | `psh/gather.py:157` | `level` | `{site},not-installed,{name}` | local map | own |\n| 8 | `psh/gather.py:174` | `level` | `{site},turned-off,{name}` | local map | own |\n| 9 | `psh/gather.py:446` | alert | `{site},composer-update` | default | own |\n| 10 | `psh/gather.py:581` | info | `{site},wp-smell,{json}` | default | own |\n| 11 | `psh/gather.py:606` | info | `{site},drush-smell,{json}` | default | own |\n| 12 | `psh/gather.py:631` | info | `{site},composer-smell,{json}` | default | own |\n| 13 | `psh/plans.py:185` | info | `{site},its-recommends-plan,{cur},{rec},{savings:.2f}` | default | own |\n| 14 | `check/addon_updates/table.py:66` | warning | `{site},updates-addons,{num}` | default | own |\n| 15 | `check/cloudflare/notices.py:397` | info | `{site},cloudflare-cache,{fqdns},{ids}` | derived | derived |\n| 16–20 | `check/dns/notices.py:25,42,77,113,123` | warning/alert | `{site},{code},` + `\",\".join(hostnames)` | derived | own |\n| 21 | `check/drupal/d7_eol.py:13` | alert | `{site},drupal7-eol` | default | own |\n| 22 | `check/pantheon/frozen.py:14` | alert | `{site},frozen` | default | own |\n| 23 | `check/pantheon/live_env.py:16` | alert | `{site},no-live-env-but-paid-plan` | default | own |\n| 24 | `check/pantheon/php_eol.py:12` | warning | `{site},php-eol-warning` | default | own |\n| 25 | `check/pantheon/php_eol.py:39` | alert | `{site},php-eol-alert` | default | own |\n| 26–28 | `check/pantheon/updates.py:59,93,129` | info/warning/alert | `{site},updates-{sev},{num},{days}` | default | own |\n| 29 | `check/pantheon_cdn_change/notices.py:197` | info | `{site},pantheon-cdn-change,` + joined fqdns | derived | own |\n| 30 | `check/umich/annual_billing.py:23` | alert | `{site},annual-bill,{amount},{shortcode}` | **CUSTOM 💵** | own |\n| 31 | `check/umich/drupal_ua.py:79` | info | `{site},drupal-ua,{result}` | default | own |\n| 32 | `check/umich/hummingbird.py:36` | info | `{site},unsupported-turned-off,{name}` | default | own |\n| 33 | `check/umich/hummingbird.py:47` | alert | `{site},unsupported,{name}` | default | own |\n| 34 | `check/umich/oidc_login.py:23` | warning | `{site},umich-oidc-login-reinstall` | default | own |\n| 35 | `check/umich/sitelens.py:106` | info | `{site},sitelens-url-paths,{num}` | derived | derived |\n| 36 | `check/wordpress/favicon.py:40` | warning | `{site},no-favicon` | default | own |\n| 37 | `check/wordpress/ocp.py:41` | alert | `{site},ocp-config-fix-needed` | default | own |\n\n**Icon arithmetic (measured, D-i14c-5):** 29 producers carry an explicit `icon`; **26** are\nliterals equal to `sc.icon[type]`, 2 are `check_drupal_module`'s local variable, 1 is the\ncustom 💵. The conversion therefore deletes **28** icon entries and keeps exactly **one** — and\nwith the 26 literals go 26 chances of a silent typo in an HTML entity that no `.ambr` snapshot\nand only one test assert.\n\n**#7/#8 lose a hand-rolled severity→icon map.** `check_drupal_module` builds\n`icon = \"&#x26A0;\"; if level == \"info\": icon = \"&#x1F50E;\"` — a duplicate of `sc.icon` that is\n*wrong* for `level=\"alert\"` (a warning triangle on an alert notice). After conversion,\n`severity=Severity(level)` derives the icon from the one map and an unknown level raises\n`ValueError` at the producer instead of shipping a wrong icon (PD#1, PD#2). **The `level`\nparameter and both of its reachable values are LIVE** — spec-review finding 3 corrected an\nearlier claim here: there are **three** callers, and `check/umich/cloudflare_cms.py:31` passes\n`level=\"info\"` for `purge_processor_cron`, pinned by\n`tests/integration/test_check_umich_cloudflare_cms.py:127`\n(`[\"info\", \"warning\", \"warning\", \"warning\"]`). `Severity(level)` reproduces both reachable\nicons exactly; only the unreachable `alert` case changes, from wrong to correct.\n\n**Terminology (PD#11).** `wp_error(site, code, message, errors)` / `drush_error(...)` take a\nparameter named `code` that is **not** a notice code — it is the failing operation\n(`\"version-check\"`, `\"plugin-list\"`, `\"ocp-config-check\"`, …) and becomes `csv_extra[0]`, while\nthe notice code is `wp-error`. Two different things named `code` in one call is the collision\nPD#11 forbids: rename the parameter to **`operation`**. All **12** call sites (7 in\n`psh/gather.py`; `check/wordpress/ocp.py:28`, `check/wordpress/favicon.py:24`,\n`check/drupal/multisite.py:29`, `check/umich/drupal_ua.py:49,64`) and both test call sites\n(`tests/integration/test_wrappers.py:85,96`) pass it **positionally** (verified), so the rename\ncosts no call-site churn beyond the two definitions.\n\n### 2.5 The two producers that do not go through `add_notice`\n\n1. **`check/umich/annual_billing.py`** publishes the hook-produced key `annual_bill_upcoming`,\n   read by `sort_notices_and_subject` and inserted straight into the render-only\n   `sorted_notices` list — never into `site_context[\"notices\"]`, so it never reaches\n   `-notices.csv` (load-bearing, LEDGER I12). The builder returns a `Notice`; the **hook**\n   publishes `site_context.notice_to_dict(notice)`, so the key's documented type (a render\n   dict) and every consumer stay unchanged. This is why the projection is public.\n2. **`psh/cli.py:no_primary_domain_notice`** returns a notice or `None`; `main()` passes the\n   non-`None` result to `add_notice`. It returns `Notice | None` after conversion; the `None`\n   branch and the call site are untouched.\n\n### 2.6 Deliverable E — retiring the dict form\n\n`add_notice` becomes `Notice`-only:\n\n- A non-`Notice` argument raises **`TypeError`**, naming the offending value's type and\n  pointing at `psh.notice.Notice` (PD#2: a named error, not a bare exit).\n- The `if 'message' not in notice: console.print(...); sys.exit(1)` guard is **deleted** — it\n  guards a dict that can no longer arrive, and `Notice.html` is a required constructor\n  argument. Its test (`tests/unit/test_site_context.py:89`,\n  `test_add_notice_missing_message_exits`, asserting `SystemExit`) is **rewritten** to assert\n  the `TypeError` on a dict argument; see §5(4).\n- `add_notices(notices: list[Notice])` is unchanged apart from its docstring/annotation.\n- `Notice.order` keeps its `\"prepend\"`/`\"first\"` semantics in `add_notice` even though no\n  producer uses them today: it is part of the §6 field set, and `add_news_item` shows the\n  behavior is live for the sibling collection.\n\n### 2.7 Decisions (D-i14c-1…11, exhaustive)\n\n| # | Decision | Why |\n|---|---|---|\n| D-i14c-1 | `csv_extra: tuple[str, ...] = ()`, joined after `site,code` | §2.1; user-selected over `csv_suffix`/`csv` override |\n| D-i14c-2 | The render dict stays the storage form; only the *producer* dict form is retired | Templates/sort/csv all read it; replacing it would move goldens (§3 bar) |\n| D-i14c-3 | The projection becomes public `SiteContext.notice_to_dict` with full normalization | One projection for both the `add_notice` path and the billing produced key (DRY); the site name cannot be mismatched because it comes from the context |\n| D-i14c-4 | `order` is dropped from the stored render dict | `add_notice` was its only reader; no producer sets it |\n| D-i14c-5 | Explicit `icon=` kept only on `annual-bill` (#30); 26 literal defaults and `check_drupal_module`'s 2-entry local map are deleted | Measured equal to the default by `tools/notice_inventory.py`; removes 26 unasserted literals and one latent wrong-icon bug |\n| D-i14c-6 | All 36 roster codes registered at import through module-level constants | Makes the duplicate-code guard real; a constant cannot drift from what was registered |\n| D-i14c-7 | `NoticeRegistry.snapshot()/restore()` + autouse `reset_sc` restore, plus the §2.3 no-load-outside-a-function-scoped-fixture rule (one test file fixed) | Standalone module loads re-execute module bodies; user-selected over an idempotent `register()`, which would silently allow a genuine duplicate |\n| D-i14c-8 | `wp_error`/`drush_error` parameter `code` → `operation` | PD#11: two different things named `code` in one call after conversion |\n| D-i14c-9 | `add_notice` raises `TypeError` on a non-`Notice`; # Writing Plans

## Overview

Write comprehensive implementation plans assuming the engineer has zero context for our codebase and questionable taste. Document everything they need to know: which files to touch for each task, code, testing, docs they might need to check, how to test it. Give them the whole plan as bite-sized tasks. DRY. YAGNI. TDD. Frequent commits.

Assume they are a skilled developer, but know almost nothing about our toolset or problem domain. Assume they don't know good test design very well.

**Announce at start:** "I'm using the writing-plans skill to create the implementation plan."

**Context:** If working in an isolated worktree, it should have been created via the `superpowers:using-git-worktrees` skill at execution time.

**Save plans to:** `docs/superpowers/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<feature-name>.md`
- (User preferences for plan location override this default)

## Scope Check

If the spec covers multiple independent subsystems, it should have been broken into sub-project specs during brainstorming. If it wasn't, suggest breaking this into separate plans — one per subsystem. Each plan should produce working, testable software on its own.

## File Structure

Before defining tasks, map out which files will be created or modified and what each one is responsible for. This is where decomposition decisions get locked in.

- Design units with clear boundaries and well-defined interfaces. Each file should have one clear responsibility.
- You reason best about code you can hold in context at once, and your edits are more reliable when files are focused. Prefer smaller, focused files over large ones that do too much.
- Files that change together should live together. Split by responsibility, not by technical layer.
- In existing codebases, follow established patterns. If the codebase uses large files, don't unilaterally restructure - but if a file you're modifying has grown unwieldy, including a split in the plan is reasonable.

This structure informs the task decomposition. Each task should produce self-contained changes that make sense independently.

## Task Right-Sizing

A task is the smallest unit that carries its own test cycle and is worth a
fresh reviewer's gate. When drawing task boundaries: fold setup,
configuration, scaffolding, and documentation steps into the task whose
deliverable needs them; split only where a reviewer could meaningfully
reject one task while approving its neighbor. Each task ends with an
independently testable deliverable.

## Bite-Sized Task Granularity

**Each step is one action (2-5 minutes):**
- "Write the failing test" - step
- "Run it to make sure it fails" - step
- "Implement the minimal code to make the test pass" - step
- "Run the tests and make sure they pass" - step
- "Commit" - step

## Plan Document Header

**Every plan MUST start with this header:**

```markdown
# [Feature Name] Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** [One sentence describing what this builds]

**Architecture:** [2-3 sentences about approach]

**Tech Stack:** [Key technologies/libraries]

## Global Constraints

[The spec's project-wide requirements — version floors, dependency limits,
naming and copy rules, platform requirements — one line each, with exact
values copied verbatim from the spec. Every task's requirements implicitly
include this section.]

---
```

## Task Structure

````markdown
### Task N: [Component Name]

**Files:**
- Create: `exact/path/to/file.py`
- Modify: `exact/path/to/existing.py:123-145`
- Test: `tests/exact/path/to/test.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: [what this task uses from earlier tasks — exact signatures]
- Produces: [what later tasks rely on — exact function names, parameter
  and return types. A task's implementer sees only their own task; this
  block is how they learn the names and types neighboring tasks use.]

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```python
def test_specific_behavior():
    result = function(input)
    assert result == expected
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run test to verify it fails**

Run: `pytest tests/path/test.py::test_name -v`
Expected: FAIL with "function not defined"

- [ ] **Step 3: Write minimal implementation**

```python
def function(input):
    return expected
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Run test to verify it passes**

Run: `pytest tests/path/test.py::test_name -v`
Expected: PASS

- [ ] **Step 5: Commit**

```bash
git add tests/path/test.py src/path/file.py
git commit -m "feat: add specific feature"
```
````

## No Placeholders

Every step must contain the actual content an engineer needs. These are **plan failures** — never write them:
- "TBD", "TODO", "implement later", "fill in details"
- "Add appropriate error handling" / "add validation" / "handle edge cases"
- "Write tests for the above" (without actual test code)
- "Similar to Task N" (repeat the code — the engineer may be reading tasks out of order)
- Steps that describe what to do without showing how (code blocks required for code steps)
- References to types, functions, or methods not defined in any task

## Remember
- Exact file paths always
- Complete code in every step — if a step changes code, show the code
- Exact commands with expected output
- DRY, YAGNI, TDD, frequent commits

## Self-Review

After writing the complete plan, look at the spec with fresh eyes and check the plan against it. This is a checklist you run yourself — not a subagent dispatch.

**1. Spec coverage:** Skim each section/requirement in the spec. Can you point to a task that implements it? List any gaps.

**2. Placeholder scan:** Search your plan for red flags — any of the patterns from the "No Placeholders" section above. Fix them.

**3. Type consistency:** Do the types, method signatures, and property names you used in later tasks match what you defined in earlier tasks? A function called `clearLayers()` in Task 3 but `clearFullLayers()` in Task 7 is a bug.

If you find issues, fix them inline. No need to re-review — just fix and move on. If you find a spec requirement with no task, add the task.

## Execution Handoff

After saving the plan, offer execution choice:

**"Plan complete and saved to `docs/superpowers/plans/<filename>.md`. Two execution options:**

**1. Subagent-Driven (recommended)** - I dispatch a fresh subagent per task, review between tasks, fast iteration

**2. Inline Execution** - Execute tasks in this session using executing-plans, batch execution with checkpoints

**Which approach?"**

**If Subagent-Driven chosen:**
- **REQUIRED SUB-SKILL:** Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development
- Fresh subagent per task + two-stage review

**If Inline Execution chosen:**
- **REQUIRED SUB-SKILL:** Use superpowers:executing-plans
- Batch execution with checkpoints for review

{name}\",\n                html=f'<p>The <a href=\"{escape_url(url)}\">…</a> …</p>',\n                text=f\"The {display_name} Drupal module\\n<{url}>\\n…\",\n            )\n        )\n```\n\nRules for the recipe:\n1. `\"type\"` → `severity=Severity.ALERT|WARNING|INFO` (or `Severity(level)` for the two\n   `check_drupal_module` producers). **Delete** `icon` unless the value differs from\n   `sc.icon[type]` — in this task every icon is the default, so all 13 `icon` entries go, and\n   `check_drupal_module`'s local `icon = \"&#x26A0;\" … if level == \"info\"` map is deleted with\n   them (SPEC §2.4).\n2. `\"csv\"` → `code=<CONSTANT>` + `csv_extra=(…)` with the same field values in the same order.\n   For `wp_error`/`drush_error`: `csv_extra=(operation, json.dumps(errors).replace(\",\", \"\\\\,\"))`.\n   For `psh/plans.py`: `csv_extra=(current_plan, recommended_plan, f\"{savings:.2f}\")`.\n   For `psh/cli.py`'s no-primary-domain: `csv_extra=(\"\",)`.\n3. `\"message\"` → `html=`, `\"short\"` → `short=`, `\"text\"` → `text=` — **paste the literal, do not\n   retype or re-wrap it**; several are column-0 `f\"\"\"` bodies (Invariant 8).\n4. ## What I verified independently (not from the report)

**Projection byte-equivalence — derived, not trusted.** I reconstructed the old `add_notice`/`_notice_to_dict` verbatim from `git show 7affff8:script_context.py` and ran the new code against it over the full cross-product of input shapes (3 severities × explicit/derived `short` × explicit/derived `text` × explicit/derived `icon` × `append`/`prepend`/`first`/unknown `order` × non-empty/empty `html`):

```
192 cases, 0 mismatches (order key excluded from comparison)
order-key storage differs for order= prepend old keys: ['type','csv','short','message','order','icon','text'] new keys: ['type','icon','csv','short','message','text']
```

So: content-identical for every shape; the only difference is the deliberate `order` key drop (D-i14c-4), which I confirmed is unobservable — repo-wide grep for `'order'`/`"order"` finds only `script_context.py:133` (the dict arm) and `:200` (`add_news_item`), and `email_template.html`/`.txt` contain no `notice.order` (only CSS `border`/`box-sizing` matches). Insertion position (`prepend`/`first` → front) is preserved in all 192 cases.

**Legacy dict arm — unchanged, including object identity.** 20-case differential (icon present/absent × text present/absent × `order` absent/`append`/`prepend`/`first`/unknown):

```
dict arm: 20 cases, 0 mismatches (incl. key insertion order + object identity)
ERROR: Notice is missing the "message" key: {'type': 'info'}
missing-message SystemExit code: 1
unknown type -> KeyError 'nope'
```

The stored object is still the caller's dict (`is` identity), so the `dict(built)` copies in `test_cachecheck_notice_render.py` / `test_pantheon_cdn_change_notice_render.py` keep working, and Tasks 2–5 rest on a genuinely untouched path.

**`snapshot`/`restore` completeness.** `/workspace/psh/notice.py:69` returns `dict(self._codes)` (copy, descriptions included) and `:73` sets `self._codes = dict(snapshot)` (replace, copy) — no aliasing either direction, no shared mutable state. `/workspace/tests/conftest.py:126` is `@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)` function-scoped and the restore at `:161` is inside `finally:`, so the failure path is covered.

**Counts and gates, re-run by me.** `git archive 7affff8` → fresh tree → `1022/1024 tests collected`; HEAD → `1029/1031`. Difference exactly 7. `./run-tests --fast` → `1028 passed, 1 skipped, 2 deselected`, `107 snapshots passed`. `uvx ruff@0.15.22 check .` → `All checks passed!`; `.venv/bin/pyright` → `0 errors, 0 warnings, 0 informations`. `git diff 982589f b199ab8 -- tests/e2e/__snapshots__/ '*.ambr'` → empty.

## Attacking the two unverifiable claims

**Claim 1 — `test_notice_projects_to_legacy_dict` passes unmodified and is not trivial. CONFIRMED, and it is a real instrument.** The diff touches only lines 26+ of `/workspace/tests/unit/test_add_notice_from_notice.py`; lines 9–21 are untouched. I proved it can go red without editing any repo file, by injecting a pytest plugin (`-p`, on `PYTHONPATH`) that corrupts `SiteContext.notice_to_dict` at runtime:

| sabotage | result |
|---|---|
| `d.pop("icon")` | FAILED — `{...'text': 'hi'} != {...'text': 'hi', 'icon': '&#x1F6A8;'}` |
| `d["csv"] += ",oops"` | FAILED — `'s1,no-domains,oops' != 's1,no-domains'` |
| `d["text"] = "WRONG"` | FAILED |
| unmodified | `1 passed` |

It survives *because* the projection now reproduces the historical fill, not because the comparison went vacuous: the Notice arm no longer runs `add_notice`'s fill logic at all, so any projection field that regresses shows up as a dict-inequality against the dict arm. Note its scope: it exercises the `icon` derivation and the empty-`csv_extra` csv join, not `html2text` derivation or `csv_extra` (both covered by siblings).

**Claim 2 — the `reset_sc` restore was proven able to go red. The one-shot proof happened, but the repository is left with an instrument that cannot go red. A permanent test is needed, and now is the right time.** See Finding 1.

## Findings

### 1. [MEDIUM — PD#14] The `reset_sc` registry restore has zero permanent coverage; deleting it today is invisible. **Fix before Task 2 lands.**

`/workspace/tests/conftest.py:161` — `notice_registry.restore(saved_codes)`.

Evidence I ran: a pytest plugin that no-ops `NoticeRegistry.restore` **only when called from `reset_sc`** (leaving the round-trip unit test's direct calls intact), then the whole non-live suite:

```
107 snapshots passed.
1028 passed, 1 skipped, 2 deselected, 4 warnings in 46.83s
```

Identical to the unmodified run. `tests/unit/test_notice.py:57` (`test_registry_snapshot_and_restore_round_trip`) tests `NoticeRegistry` on a *fresh instance* — it never touches the conftest wiring. So the seam Task 1 exists to build is, in the committed tree, a green check that has never been shown capable of going red. PD#14, verbatim: *"A green check is a claim, not evidence, until it has been shown capable of going red on the condition it guards."* The implementer's throwaway probe satisfied the *demonstration*; it does not satisfy the *guard*, and nothing in the repo records that the demonstration happened.

Two counter-arguments, and why they do not carry:
- *"SPEC §2.3 point 3 says no permanent guard is specified."* That clause reasons about the **duplicate-code hazard** being fail-loud (`DuplicateNoticeCodeError`) — it is a PD#1 argument, not a PD#14 one, and it only starts holding at Task 2 when producers actually register. Today the restore is inert, so a silent deletion has no symptom at all.
- *"Task 2 makes it load-bearing anyway."* True but the coverage is **incidental and unnamed**: it surfaces as a `DuplicateNoticeCodeError` in some unrelated check-module test, and the conftest docstring's invariant ("no producing module is executed outside a function-scoped fixture or test body") is asserted by nothing.

**Fix (verified working — I ran both states in a scratch copy of `b199ab8`, no repo file edited):**

```python
# tests/unit/test_registry_reset.py
import pytest
from psh.notice import registry

pytestmark = pytest.mark.unit


@pytest.mark.parametrize("_run", [1, 2])
def test_reset_sc_restores_the_notice_registry(_run, reset_sc):
    """Producing modules register codes at import and the suite re-loads them per test, so
    reset_sc MUST undo in-test registrations.  Two runs of the SAME registration is the only
    condition that can go red (SPEC I14c §2.3, D-i14c-7)."""
    registry.register("probe-reset-sc")
```

With the restore in place: `2 passed`. With `notice_registry.restore(saved_codes)` replaced by `pass`:

```
E   psh.notice.DuplicateNoticeCodeError: notice code 'probe-reset-sc' is already registered (existing: ''); codes must be unique.
FAILED tests/unit/test_registry_reset.py::test_reset_sc_restores_the_notice_registry[2]
1 failed, 1 passed in 0.45s
```

Parametrization (rather than two sibling test functions) makes the ordering explicit rather than dependent on file order. It can ride in Task 2's commit, but the seam is Task 1's deliverable and this is the cheapest moment.

### 2. [LOW — process, PD#9] Uncommitted edits to the ratified SPEC and PLAN sit in the working tree, where Task 6's `git add -A` will swallow them.

```
$ git status --porcelain
 M development/2026-07-24-mod-I14c-notice/PLAN.md
 M development/2026-07-24-mod-I14c-notice/SPEC.md
```

Both are the pyright venv-vs-`uvx` correction discovered at Task 1 (`SPEC.md` §8 `uvx pyright@1.1.411` → `pyright`; `PLAN.md` Global Constraints gains the matching bullet). PLAN.md line 901 is literally `git add -A`, so if these linger to Task 6 an amendment to a ratified campaign document lands inside a code commit with no ledger note — the "silent divergence" CLAUDE.md forbids ("Architecture changes are amendments: edit `CAMPAIGN.md` *and* append a `LEDGER.md` entry — never a silent divergence"). PD#9: *"Everything deferred is written down. Vague intentions are lies."*
**Fix:** commit them now as their own `docs(campaign-I14c): correct the pyright invocation in the acceptance block` commit (matching `b3ffd29`/`7affff8`), and note the correction in the I14c ledger entry alongside the "22 → 28" correction SPEC §7 already owes.

### 3. [LOW — PD#11] `Notice`'s class docstring now points at the wrong function for text derivation.

`/workspace/psh/notice.py:21-22`:

> `` `html` is the report-body HTML, `text` its plaintext (empty -> derived by SiteContext.add_notice via html2text, as the dict form does) ``

As of this commit the derivation happens in `SiteContext.notice_to_dict` (`/workspace/script_context.py:155`), not `add_notice`. Same for `icon` at `:23`. PD#11: *"Terminology stays clear and consistent — within the new design and across the existing codebase. Fix any terminology problems you find."* This also makes the report's §9 PD#8 claim — *"the docstrings that did describe the retired projection semantics … were rewritten in the same edit rather than left describing the old shape"* — inaccurate for this one.
**Fix:** `derived by SiteContext.notice_to_dict via html2text` (and drop "as the dict form does", which dies at Task 6 anyway).

### 4. [LOW — record accuracy] The one sanctioned stdout change lands at Task 1, not at Tasks 4/5, and covers one more producer than SPEC §3 enumerates.

SPEC §3's stdout row scopes the `-v` notices-dump key-order change to *"the 8 icon-less producers"*. Measured, at `7affff8` vs `b199ab8`, for the no-domains shape (explicit `text`, derived `icon`):

```
OLD (7affff8) no-domains key order: ['type', 'csv', 'short', 'message', 'text', 'icon']
NEW (b199ab8) no-domains key order: ['type', 'icon', 'csv', 'short', 'message', 'text']
```

`no-domains` is explicitly *excluded* from the 8 (SPEC §1.2: "already `Notice`-based since I3 — untouched"), yet its `psh/cli.py:879` debug dump changes key order **today**. Zero functional impact (CAMPAIGN.md §8: "stdout / console / error messages MAY improve freely"), but PD#5 wants it named rather than absent, and the task report's §7 documents the new key order without flagging that it is already an observable delta.
**Fix:** one clause in the I14c ledger entry: the §3 stdout change begins at Task 1 and includes `no-domains`.

### 5. [OPTIONAL — PD#1/PD#2, robustness] `csv_extra` element types are unenforced in the half of the tree pyright does not see.

`pyproject.toml:137` — `include = ["psh"]`. `check/**` and `script_context.py` are **outside** the type gate. Tasks 3–5 convert 20 producers in `check/` whose `csv_extra` must be pre-stringified (`(str(num_updates), str(oldest_update_days))`, PLAN Task 3 Step 3). A missed `str()` there is caught by neither pyright nor ruff; it surfaces at `/workspace/script_context.py:152` as `TypeError: sequence item 2: expected str instance, int found`, mid-run, naming neither the notice code nor the producer. Instrument I3 (one csv test per code) is the intended net and is probably sufficient — hence "optional" — but the cheap belt-and-braces is at the type, where it names the failure (PD#2: *"Every error has a name."*):

```python
    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        bad = [x for x in self.csv_extra if not isinstance(x, str)]
        if bad:
            raise TypeError(f"Notice({self.code!r}).csv_extra elements must be str; got {bad!r}")
```

This is validation, not coercion, so it does not contradict D-i14c-1's "the projection does not coerce" decision. If you decline it, say so in the ledger so Task 3's reviewer does not re-raise it.

## What I verified independently (not re-running what you already ran)

31 rows × (csv, type, icon, short, message, text)  →  IDENTICAL-INCL-BODIES  (12,425 bytes)
```

Coverage: `wp_error`/`drush_error` (stderr = `"boom"`, `"a, b\nc"`, `""`), `check_wordpress_plugin` ×3 shapes, `check_drupal_module` ×2 shapes × **both reachable levels** (`warning`, `info`), all three smell builders (incl. comma+newline stderr), `build_plan_recommendation_notice` (umich/generic/zero-savings), `no_primary_domain_notice`. This is strictly stronger than instrument I2a: `literal_equality.py` compares a **multiset** of `ast.dump` (its own docstring: *"Field RENAMES are invisible to this check by design"*), so a *swap* of two bodies within one file would report `identical`. The probe would not.

Per-producer csv derivation, field by field, all confirmed byte-equal:

| # | old literal | new `code` + `csv_extra` | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `f"{site['name']},no-primary-domain,"` | `no-primary-domain` + `("",)` | ✓ trailing empty field preserved |
| 2/3 | `f"{site},wp-error,{code},{json.dumps(errors).replace(',','\\,')}"` | + `(operation, json.dumps(errors).replace(',','\\,'))` | ✓ escaping verbatim |
| 4–8 | `f"{site},{not-installed\|multiple-installed\|turned-off},{name}"` | + `(name,)` | ✓ |
| 9 | `f"{site['name']},composer-update"` | + `()` | ✓ (see below) |
| 10–12 | `f"{site_name},{wp\|drush\|composer}-smell,{json…}"` | + `(json…,)` | ✓ |
| 13 | `…,{cur},{rec},{savings:.2f}` | + `(current_plan, recommended_plan, f"{savings:.2f}")` | ✓ `.ambr` still pins the full row `'s,its-recommends-plan,Performance Medium,Performance Small,1234.50'` |

Producer 9 is the one not directly callable; its row is pinned end-to-end at `/workspace/tests/integration/test_gather_drupal.py:172` (`== [f"{SITE_D8['name']},composer-update"]`, read through the projection), and its body lines are unmodified context lines in the diff.

**Site-name substitution (the projection now owns that half).** I read every call site of the 7 converted builders in `psh/`, `check/`, `plugin/`: all 21 pass `site["name"]` / `site_context["site"]["name"]` for the same `site` dict that `psh/cli.py:577`'s `sc.SiteContext(site)` was built from. No mismatch exists today.

**Severity/icon.** `sc.icon` = `{'info':'&#x1F50E;','warning':'&#x26A0;','alert':'&#x1F6A8;'}`; every deleted literal matched, confirmed by the probe's `icon` column. `check_drupal_module`'s local map: `Severity("warning")`→`&#x26A0;`, `Severity("info")`→`&#x1F50E;` — both reproduced; the only changed case is the unreachable `alert` (wrong→correct), and I confirmed the three callers (`check/drupal/d7_eol.py:36`, `check/drupal/papc.py:10`, `check/umich/cloudflare_cms.py:58`) pass only default or `level="info"`. `/workspace/tests/integration/test_check_umich_cloudflare_cms.py:127` is a live end-to-end pin of both.

**Rename.** `grep -rn 'wp_error(\|drush_error(' psh/ check/ tests/ | grep 'code='` → none. Both defs renamed with docstrings that state the distinction explicitly. No doc anywhere states the old parameter list.

**Registration.** 12 `registry.register(` calls, 12 distinct codes; 13 `Notice(` construction sites in `psh/` use `NOTICE_*` constants (one literal — see the open question). `not-installed`/`turned-off` registered once each in `psh/gather.py`. No test reloads or standalone-loads a `psh/` module, and `reset_sc(psh)` depends on the session-scoped `psh` fixture, so the psh registrations are inside the snapshot — no leak.

**Gates, run by me:** `uvx ruff@0.15.22 check .` → `All checks passed!`; `/workspace/.venv/bin/pyright` (1.1.411) → `0 errors, 0 warnings`; `literal_equality.py 54119f5 …` → `4/4 files with byte-identical notice literals`, and `--self-test` → `SELF-TEST PASSED … 2 changed literal(s)`.

**Not a finding, but worth recording in the ledger:** the `-v` notices dump does **not** change at Task 2. All 13 base dicts were already written in `type, icon, csv, short, message, text` order — exactly the projection's order — so SPEC §3's sanctioned stdout change stays confined to the 8 icon-less producers in Tasks 4–5.
