## Findings

### 1. The report claims two return annotations that were not made — MEDIUM, fix before Task 3

`/workspace/.superpowers/sdd/task-2-report.md:33-38` asserts as delivered:

> `check_wordpress_plugin(...) -> list[Notice]`, … `build_plan_recommendation_notice(...) -> Notice`

Actual tree:

- `/workspace/psh/gather.py:92` — `) -> list:` (unparameterized; its sibling `check_drupal_module` at `psh/gather.py:161` *did* get `-> list[Notice]`)
- `/workspace/psh/plans.py:149-151` — `def build_plan_recommendation_notice(  # noqa: PLR0913 …\n    site_name, …\n):` — **no return annotation at all**

PLAN Task 2 → *Interfaces → Produces* lists both. PD#14: *"A green check is a claim, not evidence, until it has been shown capable of going red on the condition it guards."* An assertion in a task report is the same class of instrument, and this one is simply wrong — nothing in the gates can catch it (pyright standard mode does not require return annotations).

**Fix:** `-> list[Notice]` on `psh/gather.py:92`; `-> Notice` on `psh/plans.py:151`. Then correct the report, or note the correction in the Task-3 report. Two lines, zero behavior.

### 2. `add_notices`' docstring became false at this commit — LOW, fix now (one line)

`/workspace/script_context.py:159`:

```python
        """Add each notice dict returned by a builder (wp_error/drush_error/check_*module)."""
```

Those are exactly the four builders Task 2 converted; they now return `Notice` objects. PLAN Task 6 defers the *annotation*, but the sentence naming these four builders is wrong **as of `509ee4e`**, not as of Task 6. PD#8: *"Where a diagram exists in a comment or docstring, updating it is part of changing the flow it describes; a stale diagram is worse than none."* PD#11: *"Terminology stays clear and consistent — within the new design and across the existing codebase."*

**Fix:** `"""Add each notice returned by a builder (wp_error/drush_error/check_*module) — Notice since I14c Task 2; the dict path is retired at Task 6."""`

### 3. The escaping gap is closed for 2 of the 5 copies of the escape expression — LOW, optional (4 lines)

`json.dumps(x).replace(',', '\\,')` now appears in five `csv_extra` expressions: `psh/gateway.py:230` (wp_error), `:315` (drush_error), `psh/gather.py:601, 626, 651` (the three smells). The report (§4) frames the two added tests as closing the gap left by the old `.startswith(...)` assertions. They close it for **two**:

- `tests/integration/test_wrappers.py:109` — `wp_error("s","plugin-list",…,"a, b")` → `("plugin-list", '"a\\, b"')` ✓ comma-bearing, literal expectation
- `tests/unit/test_smell_notices.py:44` — `("a, b\nc", "", "")` → `('"a\\, b\\nc"',)` ✓

The other three are not pinned against a comma: `test_wrappers.py:98` uses `"kaboom"` (`'"kaboom"'` pins `json.dumps` but not the `.replace`), and `test_smell_notices.py:27,36` assert `json.dumps("drush broke").replace(",", "\\,")` — a comma-free input, so the `.replace` is a no-op and the assertion cannot go red on it. Delete `.replace(',', '\\,')` from `drush_error`, `drush-smell` or `composer-smell` today and the suite stays green. This is the memory note *"Fix the class, not the instance"* — the implementer found the defect class and fixed one instance of it per file.

**Fix:** feed a comma to the drush/composer cases too (one extra assertion each, or parametrize). **Optional follow-up (DRY — the Spine: *"flag repetition aggressively"*):** five copies of one escaping rule with no shared name is a good candidate for a `csv_field(s)` helper in `psh/notice.py`, tested once — but that is a design change, so it belongs in a spec amendment, not in Task 3.

### 4. Observation, no action required

`tests/integration/test_wrappers.py` dropped `n["csv"].startswith("its-wws-test1,wp-error,PLUGIN_FAIL,")`, so the `site` argument of `wp_error`/`drush_error` and the site name the projection uses are now two independent sources with no cross-check anywhere in the suite. I verified all call sites agree, and the general property is pinned by Task 1's `test_add_notice_from_notice.py`, so this is not a defect — recording it because it is the one degree of freedom the conversion opened.

---

## Open question: `psh/cli.py:140` / `:670` — should Task 6 unify `no-domains`?

**Yes, Task 6 should unify it.** The two idioms now sit two lines apart:

```python
# psh/cli.py:140-142
registry.register("no-domains", description="paid plan with no custom domains connected")
NOTICE_NO_PRIMARY_DOMAIN = registry.register(
    "no-primary-domain", description="multiple custom domains, none primary")
```

with `code="no-domains"` re-typed at `psh/cli.py:670`. SPEC §1.2 ("untouched") and D-i14c-6 ("**All 36** roster codes registered at import through module-level constants … A constant, not a bare `register(...)` call plus a literal at the construction site, so the code used **cannot** drift from the code registered (PD#1)") are in direct tension, and §1.2's "untouched" was written about *converting a dict producer* — `no-domains` is already a `Notice`, so the exemption doesn't actually reach the idiom.

The reason to unify is not tidiness. The roster test (I4) compares `registry.codes()` against the frozen roster — it reads only the **registration** side. A typo in the *construction-site* literal (`code="no-domain"`) would leave the roster green and ship a wrong `-notices.csv` code: PD#1, *"A failure that can happen silently is a critical defect."* That is precisely the hole D-i14c-6 exists to close, and `no-domains` is the one code left standing in it — in the same file where a future reader will copy whichever pattern they see first.

Cost: two lines (`NOTICE_NO_DOMAINS = registry.register(...)`, `code=NOTICE_NO_DOMAINS`), no test change, and the e2e goldens that render `no-domains` are the cover. Do it in Task 6 (which already edits `psh/cli.py`'s neighbourhood via Deliverable E and the docs), and note the §1.2 deviation in the ledger entry so the SPEC's "untouched" line is corrected rather than silently contradicted.

---

## Must fix before Task 3

Finding 1 (two return annotations + the report correction) and finding 2 (the `add_notices` docstring). Both are one-to-two-line edits and neither blocks Task 3 functionally — but both are claims-vs-tree mismatches, which is the defect class this increment is being reviewed for. Finding 3 is optional; finding 4 needs nothing.

- **PD#1** — *"Every failure mode must be visible… A failure that can happen silently is a critical defect."* → the `no-domains` recommendation; the deleted `check_drupal_module` icon map.

- **Engineering Preferences, DRY** — *"flag repetition aggressively"* → finding 3's optional follow-up.

## What I independently verified (not accepted from the reports)

**1. Differential probe — all 24 `check/` producers, both trees, render dict compared field-by-field.**
I built `15affb8` and `7fe92fe` into separate trees, drove every producer (hooks and pure builders) with identical stubbed inputs in each, added the result through each tree's own `SiteContext.add_notice` — the storage form both trees share — and diffed the resulting render dicts:

```
scenarios: 30      notices compared: 30      PROBLEMS: 0
```

30 scenarios because I exercised the copy variants too (dns umich/generic ×2, cdn-change umich×before_cutoff ×4, cloudflare umich ×2). Every `type`, `icon`, `csv`, `short`, `message`, `text` is byte-identical, including all the shapes the dispatch flagged:

```

**The probe was shown able to go red** (PD#14). I injected three defects into the head tree — dropped one `csv_extra` field, swapped `Severity.ALERT`→`WARNING`, changed the 💵 icon — and it reported exactly 4 differences across the three classes (csv / type / icon), then reported 0 again on a clean re-extract. I also confirmed the copy variants are genuinely distinct (4 of 4 dns umich/generic bodies differ, 2 of 2 cloudflare, 3 of 4 cdn-change), so the comparison is not degenerate.

## Findings

### 1. `sitelens-url-paths`'s **severity** is unpinned — the one field the conversion actually rewrote there — MEDIUM · **fold into Task 6**

`/workspace/tests/integration/test_check_sitelens.py:90-118` is the only test file in the suite that touches this code, and it asserts only `short` and (new) `csv`:

```
$ grep -n '"type"\|"icon"\|severity' tests/integration/test_check_sitelens.py
NONE
```

I checked all 24 codes for a `type`/`severity`/`icon` assertion anywhere in `tests/`; `sitelens-url-paths` is the **only** one with zero. So Task 5's rewrite of `'type': 'info'` → `severity=sc.Severity.INFO` at `/workspace/check/umich/sitelens.py:115` had no red-capable guard: a `Severity.WARNING` there would have changed the icon, the notice sort position, and potentially the email subject prefix (`Action Recommended: …`), and every test would still be green. My probe confirms the value *is* `info`, so there is no live defect — but the pin Task 5 added closes the csv half of the gap SPEC §4 named and leaves the half its own change touched.

This is the same shape as Task 5's own (good) `test_upcoming_notice_keeps_its_custom_banknote_icon` reasoning applied one file over.

**Fix:** one line in the existing `test_urls_notice_when_too_few_paths`:
```python
    assert ctx["notices"][0]["type"] == "info"
```

---

### 2. `check/cloudflare/notices.py:338` — one `# noqa: ARG001` silences three parameters — MEDIUM · **fold into Task 6**

```python
        site_name: str, items_by_fqdn: dict, *, umich: bool,  # noqa: ARG001 -- site_name went unused at I14c …
```

A line-scoped `noqa` suppresses every diagnostic reported on that line, so `items_by_fqdn` and `umich` are now un-gated for ARG001 too, despite the comment claiming to cover only `site_name`. Demonstrated:

```
$ cat argtest.py
def f(
        a: int, b: int, *, c: int,  # noqa: ARG001 -- only meant for `a`
        d: int) -> int:
    return d
$ uvx ruff@0.15.22 check --isolated --select ARG argtest.py
All checks passed!          # three unused args, zero diagnostics
```

(ARG001 itself is live — removing the noqa at `check/dns/notices.py:158` produces `ARG001 Unused function argument: site_name`, so the suppression is doing real work, just too much of it.)

Two adjacent problems on the same signature: the pre-existing justification at `/workspace/check/cloudflare/notices.py:334-337` — *"restructuring would relocate the same six names, not remove them"* — is now **false**, because one of the six is removable; and with `site_name` gone the function drops to five parameters, at or below ruff's `max-args = 5`, so the `PLR0913` suppression retires as well.

**Fix (recommended): drop `site_name` from `build_cache_notices`.** There are exactly six call sites (`check/cloudflare/cache.py:275`, `test_cachecheck_consolidation.py:51,158,531`, `test_cachecheck_notice_render.py:56,94`), all passing it positionally as the first argument. That removes a dead parameter, one blanket suppression, one now-false justification, and probably the `PLR0913` noqa.
**Minimum fix if it is not dropped:** put `site_name: str,` on its own line with the noqa, and correct the "same six names" sentence.

---

### 3. `psh/cli.py:668` uses a bare `code="no-domains"` literal, not the registered constant — LOW · **fold into Task 6**

```python
registry.register("no-domains", description="paid plan with no custom domains connected")   # psh/cli.py:140
NOTICE_NO_PRIMARY_DOMAIN = registry.register(…)                                             # :141
...
                            code="no-domains",                                              # :668
```

It is the only one of the 36 in this shape — my AST scan found 36 register calls, 35 bound to a `NOTICE_*` constant and zero other bare-literal `code=` sites. This is precisely what SPEC §2.3 / D-i14c-6 forbid:

> "A constant, not a bare `register(...)` call plus a literal at the construction site, so the code used **cannot** drift from the code registered (PD#1)."

And the drift is genuinely unguarded: the Task-6 roster test compares *registered* codes, so a typo at :668 leaves it green; the e2e goldens render the notice body, not its code; and the only `no-domains` assertions in `tests/` (`test_add_notice_from_notice.py:13,18`, `test_run_state.py:28,33`) construct their own literal rather than reading the producer's. This is Task 2's file and SPEC §1.2 said `no-domains` is "untouched", so it is arguably out of my range — but it is the one place the increment's own invariant does not hold, and Task 6 is the increment's consistency pass.

**Fix:** `NOTICE_NO_DOMAINS = registry.register("no-domains", …)` at :140 and `code=NOTICE_NO_DOMAINS` at :668.

---

### 4. `literal_equality.py` counts a zero-literal file as a pass — LOW · **fold into Task 6**

`compare()` prints `identical  <path>  (N notice literals)` and tallies it into `N/N` regardless of whether `N` is zero. Running Task 6's own acceptance command line produces:

```
$ python …/literal_equality.py 982589f $(git diff --name-only 982589f -- 'psh/*.py' 'check/**/*.py')
…
identical  psh/notice.py  (0 notice literals)
…
21/21 files with byte-identical notice literals
```

`psh/notice.py` legitimately has no notice literals — but the line is indistinguishable from the failure mode Task 3 actually hit and fixed (the tool blind to a file's literals while printing a confident verdict). Tasks 4 and 5 both had to eyeball the counts manually and say so in their reports; that is a human step standing in for a missing assertion. PD#14: *"A green check is a claim, not evidence, until it has been shown capable of going red on the condition it guards."*

**Fix:** in `compare()`, when `sum(before.values()) == 0`, print a distinct `NO LITERALS  <path>` line and exclude it from the `N/N` tally (or fail). One `if`.

---

### 5. `check/addon_updates/table.py:5` docstring still says "notice dict" — LOW · **fold into Task 6**

```
…the table-row / bullet-list f-string builders,
and the `updates-addons` notice dict.  Renames only …
```

The module no longer contains one. Task 4 explicitly hunted this class of prose in its own three files under PD#8 ("Where a diagram exists in a comment or docstring, updating it is part of changing the flow it describes") and fixed `check/dns/notices.py`, `check/cloudflare/notices.py` and `check/pantheon_cdn_change/notices.py`; Task 3's PD#8 section checked only for ASCII diagrams and so missed this. It reads as history-of-the-I10-move, which is why it survived — but a fresh reader greps "notice dict" and lands on a file that has none. This is the only surviving instance across all 16 converted files.

**Fix:** "…and the `updates-addons` notice (a hand-built dict until I14c)."

## Adjudications requested (recommendations, not defects)

### Item 6 — the five now-unused `site_name` parameters

There are **five**, not six: `check/dns/notices.py:48,88,119,158` and `check/cloudflare/notices.py:338` (`not_in_dns_notice` still interpolates `site_name` into both bodies, correctly carrying no noqa).

**Recommendation for Task 6 — split the decision:**

- **`check/dns/notices.py` ×4: KEEP them.** Not because the hook dispatches generically (it does not — `check/dns/hook.py:26-40` calls each builder by name), but for a stronger reason: `not_in_dns_notice` *genuinely* needs `site_name`, so dropping it from the other four would make one member of a five-builder family silently different from its siblings while all five are still called `f(site, hostnames…)` at the same seam. The uniform signature is the honest shape, and the noqa reason at :48 (referenced by the other three) already states exactly why. Cost of removal would be ~20 call-site edits in the hook and two test files for zero behavioral gain.
- **`check/cloudflare/notices.py:338`: DROP it.** No sibling family, one production call site, five test call sites, all positional. Dropping it removes a dead parameter, a blanket suppression, a false justification and probably `PLR0913` (finding 2).

### Item 7 — `check/pantheon_cdn_change/notices.py`'s direct `psh.notice` import

**Recommendation: keep the direct import; do NOT rewrite `test_notices_module_is_pure`.** Measured cost:

```
import psh.notice        →  18 modules added (all stdlib: dataclasses, enum, ast, inspect, …)
import script_context    → 276 modules added, incl. sqlalchemy, rich, html2text, the whole psh package
```

Repointing this module to `sc.registry` would pull 276 modules into a file whose whole design premise is "PURE: imports ONLY html + .model". The test's narrow *stated* intent (no dnspython, no terminus) would survive a rewritten assertion — but the module's actual, valuable property would not, and the current whitelist form (`imported == {"html"}`) is the shape the Spine's quality bar prefers over an open-ended denylist. The implementer's option (b) was the right call and the reason is already recorded in the module docstring.

**One thing Task 6 must add:** this is a standing exception to CAMPAIGN.md §3.5 ("checks and plugins import **only** `sc`"), and CLAUDE.md is explicit that *"Architecture changes are amendments: edit `CAMPAIGN.md` **and** append a `LEDGER.md` entry — never a silent divergence."* A docstring note is not the amendment channel. Record it in both, naming this one module and the purity test as the reason.

Increment I3 of the [[modularization-campaign]] (same import-back pattern as [[gateway-extraction]]).

**Config engine → `psh/configuration.py`.** `config_substitution`, the DEFER machinery
(`_DEFER_TAG` + the two compiled regexes), `process_config`, `gate_disabled_sections`,
`load_news_items`, `umich_enabled`, `cloudflare_enabled` moved out of `psh/_legacy.py` into a
new gated `psh/configuration.py`, re-imported back — so `psh.process_config` etc. and the
`sc.umich_enabled`/`sc.cloudflare_enabled` exposure assignments resolve unchanged and the ~11
existing tests calling `psh.<fn>` needed no repoint (no seam trap here: nothing patches
`config_substitution` and relies on `process_config` invoking the patched version).

**New `psh/notice.py`** — the typed replacement for the ad-hoc notice dict: `Severity`
(`StrEnum` alert/warning/info), `Notice` (frozen dataclass: `severity, code, html, short,
text, icon, order` — **no `csv` field yet**), `NoticeRegistry` (declare-once, `.register(code)`
raises `DuplicateNoticeCodeError` on a repeat), and a module-level `registry`. Pure/stdlib-only
(imports nothing from `script_context`), so both `sc` and every `psh/` module import it without
a cycle. The registry is import-time-once metadata (like `sc.substitutions`/`sc.hooks`), not
per-run state.

**`SiteContext.add_notice` now accepts a `Notice` OR the legacy dict.** A `Notice` is projected
to the exact legacy dict by `_notice_to_dict` (csv built as `{site},{code}`; `order` omitted
when `"append"`), then the existing fill logic runs — so a `Notice` and the equivalent dict
produce byte-identical stored notices. **`sc.Notice`/`sc.Severity` reach `sc` via a plain
`from psh.notice import Notice, Severity` at the top of `script_context.py`** (module-level
names become module attributes) — NOT via `sc.Notice = Notice` assignments in `_legacy.py`.
Dict form retired at I14; adoption is per-increment.

**Notice adoption is incremental.** I3 converted only `no-domains` (core, in 3 goldens) as a
PoC. A notice whose csv needs **extra** fields (e.g. `turned-off,{name}`, the
`its-recommends-plan` savings) stays a dict until the first increment that adopts it, which
MUST amend CAMPAIGN.md §6 to add a `csv_extra` field via its own ledger entry — never silently
widen `Notice`.

It projects through the public **`SiteContext.notice_to_dict(notice)`**,\nwhich is the ONE place a Notice becomes the six-key *render dict* — `{type, icon, csv, short,\nmessage, text}` — that stays the storage form in `site_context[\"notices\"]`, because\n`email_template.{html,txt}`, `sort_notices_and_subject` and `RunState.record_site_notices` all\nread it. The projection builds the csv row as `\",\".join([site_name, code, *csv_extra])`, fills\n`icon` from `severity` and `text` via html2text, and does **not** store `order`. The site name\ncomes from the SiteContext, never from the producer — builders that still take a `site_name`\nparameter use it only in their console/body text (four in `check/dns/notices.py` keep an unused\none for a uniform family signature, with `# noqa: ARG001`).\n\n**`csv_extra: tuple[str, ...]`** carries the csv fields after `site,code` (the CAMPAIGN.md §6\namendment I3 reserved and I7/I10/I12 deferred). `from psh.notice import (Notice, Severity, registry)` (the I3 mechanism: a module-level import
makes it a module attribute, so `sc.registry` exists automatically — no `sc.registry = …`
assignment). All **15** check modules that had a direct `from psh.notice import registry` are
repointed to `sc.registry.register(...)`, and each one's "registry comes from psh.notice
because sc.registry does not exist yet — I14c Task 6 adds it" comment now records the finished
state instead:

places a reader of that file cannot miss — a bolded paragraph in the module docstring ("THE ONE
SANCTIONED EXCEPTION to CAMPAIGN.md section 3.5 … Do NOT 'fix' this to sc.* — the purity test
will go red and tell you so", carrying both the `test_notices_module_is_pure` reason and the
276-vs-18 module measurement), a trailing comment on the import line
(`# NOT sc.*: the purity exception`), and a sentence at its `registry.register(...)` call.

`tests/unit/test_house_rules.py`'s `SC_FACADE_NAMES`, which
`test_documented_sc_facade_names_exist` pins. Red demonstration in §3.

## Findings

### 1. Six of the 36 codes had their severity rewritten with no assertion anywhere — and the tree claims otherwise. *(Testing / PD#14; convergence signal)* — **fix before the increment closes**

`tests/integration/test_check_sitelens.py:99-101` says:

&gt; `# Severity pinned at campaign I14c Task 6: sitelens-url-paths was the only notice code`
&gt; `# with no type/severity/icon assertion anywhere in the suite`

and commit `b619b7d` repeats it verbatim. Both are false. I swept every `["type"]` / `.severity` / `["icon"]` assertion in `tests/`, mapped it to a code, and cross-checked the four e2e goldens (none of these notices appear in any golden — `grep -c` for `composer`, `smell`, `Extended` is `0` in all four `.ambr`, and the only `primary` hit is `.pill-primary` CSS at `test_golden.ambr:200`). Six codes have **no** severity, type, or icon assertion in the entire suite:

| code | producer | severity the conversion rewrote |
|---|---|---|
| `composer-update` | `psh/gather.py:465` | `"type": "alert"` → `severity=Severity.ALERT` |
| `wp-smell` | `psh/gather.py:592` | info |
| `drush-smell` | `psh/gather.py:619` | info |
| `composer-smell` | `psh/gather.py:646` | info |
| `no-primary-domain` | `psh/cli.py:305` | info |
| `drupal7-eol` | `check/drupal/d7_eol.py:22` | alert |

Evidence for two of them: `tests/integration/test_gather_drupal.py:172` is the only non-roster reference to `composer-update` and asserts `[n["csv"] for n in ctx["notices"]] == [f"{SITE_D8['name']},composer-update"]` — csv only. `tests/integration/test_check_drupal.py:191-193` and `tests/integration/test_drupal_notice_render.py:57-60` are the only `drupal7-eol` references, and both read `csv` / `message` / `text` / `short`.

Why it matters beyond the icon: `severity` drives `sort_notices_and_subject` (`psh/cli.py:347-364`), i.e. the notice's position **and the email subject prefix** — `drupal7-eol` silently demoted to `warning` turns "Action Required" into "Action Recommended" on a real report, with every test still green.

Root cause is the SPEC's own measurement at §4 line 440: *"every other code appears in at least one test file"* — appearing in a file is not being asserted. That is exactly the instrument PD#14 forbids: **"A green check is a claim, not evidence, until it has been shown capable of going red on the condition it guards."**

This is a **convergence signal**: the Task-6 review found this class, fixed one instance, and declared the class closed. The repo's own memory note `fix-the-class-not-the-instance.md` — *"when review names a defect, grep for every instance of that defect class before re-submitting"* — is the rule that was not applied.

**Fix:** six one-line assertions where each notice is already exercised — `assert ctx["notices"][0]["type"] == "alert"` in `test_gather_drupal.py::test_d8_composer_dry_run_fatal_adds_alert_and_no_smell` (the test name already promises it) and in `test_check_drupal.py::test_drupal_7_gets_eol_alert_and_tag1_delegation` (likewise); `assert n.severity == "info"` in the three `tests/unit/test_smell_notices.py` `*_alone` tests; `assert notice.severity == "info"` in `tests/unit/test_no_primary_domain_notice.py::test_gate_true_returns_the_notice`. Then correct or delete the false "only one" claim at `tests/integration/test_check_sitelens.py:99-101`.

### 4. `notice_inventory.py --gate` does not enforce the contract its docstring states. *(Instruments / PD#14)* — **fix before the increment closes** (3 lines)

`tools/notice_inventory.py:15`: *"`--gate` exits 1 unless exactly **ONE** dict-form producer remains: script_context.py's projection."* The implementation at line 93 is:

```python
remaining = [p for p in found if not p["where"].startswith("script_context.py:")]
```

which excludes **every** dict-with-a-`csv`-key in `script_context.py`, unconditionally — not one. A second hand-built render dict added to `script_context.py` (the single most likely place for one, since that is where the projection lives) passes the gate silently. This is the third defect in this instrument family, after the `ast.Name`-only blindness and the zero-literal-counts-as-pass shape — and it is the same failure mode: the tool prints a verdict it has not actually checked.

**Fix:**
```python
projection = [p for p in found if p["where"].startswith("script_context.py:")]
remaining  = [p for p in found if p not in projection]
if len(projection) != 1:
    print(f"EXPECTED exactly 1 projection dict in script_context.py, found {len(projection)}")
    return 1
```

Secondary, **ledger it for I14d**: `literal_equality.py:18-19` discloses that "field RENAMES are invisible to this check by design", but the multiset is taken over `message|text|short` / `html|text|short` *combined per file* — so a producer whose `html` and `text` bodies were **swapped** also compares equal. That is a superset of "rename" and is not named. It is covered in practice by the `.ambr`/golden pins for every notice that has one, but say so rather than leaving the reader to infer the boundary.

---

### 5. `psh/notice.py`'s module docstring — one of the five public surfaces — omits the sanctioned direct-import exception. *(Documentation)* — **fix before the increment closes** (one clause)

`psh/notice.py:3-5`: *"Pure: imports nothing from script_context, so the sc facade and every psh/ module can import it without a cycle; **checks/plugins reach Notice/Severity via sc.**"* That is no longer universally true: `check/pantheon_cdn_change/notices.py:36` does `from psh.notice import Notice, Severity, registry`, deliberately. The exception is documented in that module's own docstring and in CLAUDE.md's Notices-vs-news bullet, but a reader arriving at the type's definition — the natural entry point — is told a rule the tree violates. PD#8's spirit ("a stale diagram is worse than none") applies to the invariant note that sits where the type is defined.

**Fix:** append "— with one sanctioned exception, `check/pantheon_cdn_change/notices.py`, whose purity test pins its imported-module set (CAMPAIGN.md §3.5)."

### 6. `Notice` validates `csv_extra` element types but not `severity`, on identical reasoning. *(Consistency / PD#2)* — **ledger it for I14d**

`psh/notice.py:39-46` justifies `__post_init__` as:

&gt; "Most producers live in check/, which pyright does not gate … so a forgotten `str()` around an int csv field would otherwise surface much later as an anonymous `TypeError: sequence item 2: expected str instance, int found` from script_context's `",".join` -- naming neither the notice nor the module (PD#2)."

The same argument holds verbatim for `severity`, which the increment rewrote at all 37 producers: a `severity="warn"` from an ungated `check/` module surfaces as an anonymous `KeyError: 'warn'` out of `script_context.notice_to_dict`'s `icon[notice.severity]`, naming neither the notice nor the module. Today every producer passes an enum member (a typo'd `Severity.WARN` is a loud `AttributeError` at import), so this is latent, not live — but the asymmetry is the kind PD#10 asks about ("optimize for the 6-month future"). Two extra lines in the same `__post_init__` (`if not isinstance(self.severity, Severity): raise TypeError(f"Notice({self.code!r}).severity must be a Severity; got {self.severity!r}")`) close it.

Related, same bucket: nothing structurally requires a `Notice.code` to be *registered*. D-i14c-6's guarantee ("a constant cannot drift from what was registered") holds for the 37 converted producers by construction, but a future producer writing `code="whatever"` with no `register()` call would pass every test — the roster test only compares the registry against `ROSTER`, and an unregistered code never enters the registry. Worth naming in the ledger as the residual, since CLAUDE.md:485-489 states the rule as if it were enforced.

---

### 7. The registration comment block is 17 near-identical copies, and the idiom drifted between tasks. *(DRY / consistency)* — **ledger it for I14d**

The Spine: *"**DRY** — flag repetition aggressively."* Seventeen `check/` and `psh/` modules carry a 4–6-line comment whose only per-file content is the code list — ~75 lines of duplicated prose, now that CLAUDE.md:483-492 carries the full rationale. Two concrete drifts a reader trips over:

- The sentence *"and a second register() of the same code raises DuplicateNoticeCodeError"* appears in every multi-code module **and** in two single-code ones (`check/cloudflare/notices.py:29`, `check/pantheon_cdn_change/notices.py:42` — both Task 4), but in none of the nine other single-code modules (Tasks 3/5). The rule looks like "multi-code modules get it" until Task 4's two files break it.
- Every `check/` copy ends *"added at I14c Task 6"* — accurate for the façade name, but on a file whose registration block landed at Task 3, 4, or 5 it reads as if the whole block did.

Also in this bucket: `NOTICE_*` constant names are the code upper-cased with `-`→`_` **except** three abbreviations (`NOTICE_NO_LIVE_ENV` for `no-live-env-but-paid-plan`, `NOTICE_PROXIED_IN_MULTIPLE_ZONES` for `proxied-in-multiple-cloudflare-zones`, `NOTICE_OCP_CONFIG` for `ocp-config-fix-needed`). Harmless — the literal sits on the next line — but it is a convention stated nowhere.

**Fix (I14d, with the CLAUDE.md wholesale rewrite):** collapse each block to one line pointing at CLAUDE.md § Notices vs. news, keeping only the file-specific sentence (`psh/gather.py`'s "registered ONCE even though both builders emit them", `check/pantheon_cdn_change/notices.py`'s exception note).

### 10. A session-cached module load is a shape the §2.3 invariant does not name. *(Testing / latent)* — **ledger it for I14d**

`tests/conftest.py:132-136` and CLAUDE.md state the invariant as "no producing module is executed outside a function-scoped fixture or test body". `tests/unit/test_cachecheck_consolidation.py:17-27` satisfies it literally — the `exec_module` happens inside the function-scoped `notices` fixture — but stashes the result in a module-level `_CACHED` dict, so `check/cloudflare/notices.py` is executed exactly once per *session*, inside whichever test happens to be first. Loading the real `check.cloudflare` package also registers `cloudflare-cache` (`check/cloudflare/__init__.py:25` → `check/cloudflare/cache.py:43` → `.notices`), so a future test that both loads the package and uses this fixture gets `DuplicateNoticeCodeError`.

It fails loud, so PD#1 is satisfied and nothing needs changing today — but the stated invariant is *necessary, not sufficient*, and this repo's history (the second `sitecustomize.py`; `ALL_PACKAGES` drifting for three increments) is exactly about rules that were true when written. Restate the invariant as "…and no producing module may be cached across tests", or drop `_CACHED`.

---

`psh/gather.py:161-165` documents the improvement — *"an unknown level raises ValueError HERE, at the producer -- campaign I14c replaced a hand-rolled 2-entry severity-&gt;icon map that silently shipped a warning triangle on an alert notice"* — and SPEC §2.4 cites PD#1/PD#2 for it. PD#2 requires naming "what triggers it, what catches it, what the operator/user sees, **and whether it's tested**"; nothing asserts it. The reachable `"info"` path *is* covered end-to-end (`tests/integration/test_check_umich_cloudflare_cms.py:129`, `sorted(n["type"] …) == ["info", "warning", "warning", "warning"]`), so this is a one-line `pytest.raises(ValueError)` away from complete.

## What landed

The `Notice` dict form is retired. All **37** notice producers across 20 files now construct a typed `psh.notice.Notice`; `SiteContext.add_notice` accepts nothing else. The reserved CAMPAIGN.md §6 amendment landed as `csv_extra: tuple[str, ...]` — so the csv row's site field now comes from the `SiteContext` and can no longer be mismatched by a producer. All 36 notice codes register at import through `NOTICE_*` constants, pinned by a new roster test, which makes `NoticeRegistry`'s duplicate-code guard load-bearing rather than the dead façade surface §17 Q4 was going to flag.

**Acceptance:** full suite with the live tier — 1055 passed / 1 skipped, 107 snapshots, both gates, EXIT=0. Four e2e goldens byte-identical across the whole increment. The only snapshot change anywhere is the 7 `'icon'` lines that SPEC §3 enumerated in advance, zero deletions.

## What the increment found

Beyond the specified work, six defects surfaced — each caught by an instrument or a review rather than by luck:

- `check_drupal_module`'s hand-rolled severity→icon map would have shipped a warning triangle on an `alert`. Both reachable levels are byte-preserved; the unreachable one goes from wrong to correct.
- A test fake had been returning a dict where the real builder returns `Notice`s — green but wrong-shaped for four tasks. The retirement is what exposed it.
- A `# noqa: ARG001` was silently covering two parameters beyond the one its comment named.
- A test loaded a producing module at import time, which would have made the registry restore un-undoable.
- **Three defects in my own two instruments**: one couldn't see `sc.Notice(...)` calls at all while printing "identical" for every converted `check/` file; one counted a zero-literal file as a pass; and `--gate` didn't enforce the contract its docstring stated. Same failure mode each time — a tool printing a verdict it hadn't checked.
- The one worth carrying forward: the Tasks-3–5 review named a defect class (a rewritten severity with no assertion), Task 6 fixed one instance and declared the class closed, and the whole-branch review measured **six more**. Severity drives the email subject prefix, so a silent demotion turns "Action Required" into "Action Recommended" on a real report with every test green. All six are now pinned and shown red. The root cause was in the spec's own measurement — "every code appears in at least one test file" is not the same as being asserted by one.

Seven non-blocking findings are ledgered to I14d, including one worth knowing now: nothing structurally requires a `Notice.code` to be *registered*, so a future producer writing an unregistered code would pass every test — CLAUDE.md currently states that rule as if it were enforced.

I have not run `/archive-session` — that's the campaign's last step per §12 and it's yours to trigger.

# Archiving a development session

Run this at the end of a feature-development session, after the work is done but
**before committing**. It populates the feature's `development/<YYYY-MM-DD-slug>/`
folder with the record artifacts, then hands back to the user to commit.

Do the deterministic, secret-sensitive work (rendering, scrubbing, token math) via
`development/finalize-session.py` — never eyeball-redact a transcript yourself. This
skill's job is only to (a) figure out the target folder, (b) capture the two things
the script can't reach from outside a live session, and (c) run the script.

## Steps

1. **Determine the target folder.** It is `development/<YYYY-MM-DD-slug>/` for the
   feature just built. If the user passed a slug as the argument, use
   `development/<that>/`. Otherwise infer it from the active `SPEC.md`, the git branch,
   or the feature under discussion; if it's ambiguous, ask the user. Create the folder
   if it doesn't exist. If prompts weren't saved yet, remind the user that
   `NN-*.prompt.md` files and any `design-notes/` are part of the record.

2. **Capture in-session-only data to a gitignored `*.raw.txt` file** in the folder:
   - **/usage (dollar cost)** — you **cannot** invoke `/usage` yourself (it's a
     built-in CLI command, not a tool). Ask the user to run `/usage` and paste its
     output, then save that verbatim to `<dir>/usage.raw.txt`. This is the source of
     the cost figure — the script has no price table. If the user skips it, proceed;
     the cost section will just say it wasn't captured.

   The `.raw.txt` file is gitignored; the script folds its contents into
   `statistics.md`.

3. **Run the script.** From the repo root:
   ```
   python development/finalize-session.py --dir development/<YYYY-MM-DD-slug> \
       [--usage-capture development/<YYYY-MM-DD-slug>/usage.raw.txt] \
       [--label NN]
   ```
   It defaults to the newest session JSONL under `~/.claude/projects/-workspace/`.
   Pass `--label NN` only for a feature spanning multiple sessions (produces
   `transcript-NN.md` / `statistics-NN.md`). Omit `--usage-capture` if you didn't create it.
   This writes `transcript.md` (scrubbed, committed), `transcript.raw.md` (gitignored),
   and `statistics.md` (committed).

4. **Scaffold `analytics.md`** in the folder if it doesn't already exist — a short
   template for the user's own narrative analysis (distinct from the auto-generated
   `statistics.md`, which is machine-written; do not hand-edit `statistics.md`):
   ```markdown
   # Analytics — <feature>

   _Your narrative: what went well, what to do differently, decisions worth remembering._
   ```

5. **Verify and report — do NOT commit.** Confirm `transcript.md` scrubbed cleanly
   with a **value-shaped** grep (matches actual secret VALUES, not prose mentions of
   the names — a session that merely discusses `SMTP_PASSWORD` etc. is fine):
   ```
   grep -nE '(SMTP_PASSWORD|AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID|AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY|CLOUDFLARE_API_KEY|CLOUDFLARE_EMAIL)[[:space:]]*[=:][[:space:]]*[^[:space:]«]|AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}|-----BEGIN [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY-----|Bearer [A-Za-z0-9._-]{12,}' development/<dir>/transcript.md
   ```
   This should return nothing; any hit is a real secret value that slipped the regex —
   surface it, don't commit. (A bare quoted word that happens to look like a token is
   left alone by design.) List what was written. Then tell the user the folder is ready
   and that they can ask you to commit everything (code + `development/<dir>/`) in one
   commit, with you writing the commit message.
